**Volodymyr Oleksandrovych Zelenskyy**[^cite_note-4] (born 25 January 1978) is a Ukrainian politician and former entertainer who has served as the sixth [president of Ukraine](20688479.md) since 2019. He took office five years after the start of the [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md) with [Russia's annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation) and [invasion of the Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas), and has continued to serve during the full-scale [Russian invasion of Ukraine](70149799.md), which has been ongoing since February 2022.

Born in [Kryvyi Rih](624764.md) into a [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language_in_Ukraine)-speaking [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Ukraine) family, Zelenskyy obtained a law degree at the city's [Institute of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryvyi_Rih_Institute_of_Economics), but instead pursued a career in comedy and entertainment. Zelenskyy co-founded the production company [Kvartal 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kvartal_95), which produced films, cartoons, and television shows including the television series *[Servant of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People_\(2015_TV_series\))*, in which he played a fictional Ukrainian president. The series aired from 2015 to 2019 and was immensely popular. [A political party](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People) with the same name as the television show was founded in March 2018 by Zelenskyy and other associates of Kvartal 95. During his campaign in the [2019 presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_presidential_election), Zelenskyy positioned himself as [anti-establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-establishment) and [anti-corruption](58111406.md), and had already been a frontrunner in [opinion polls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opinion_polling_for_the_2019_Ukrainian_presidential_election) months before he declared his candidacy. He won the election with 73.23 percent of the vote in the second round, defeating incumbent president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) in the biggest landslide in the history of [Ukrainian presidential elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_presidential_elections). His party won a landslide victory in [the snap parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election) held shortly after [his inauguration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy).&#x20;

As president, Zelenskyy has been a proponent of [e-government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/E-government), and of unity between the [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language)- and Russian-speaking parts of [the country's population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-Hosa_2019-5]<sup>: 11–13 </sup> He makes extensive use of [social media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_media_use_in_politics).[^cite_note-Hosa_2019-5]<sup>: 7–10 </sup> During the first two years of his presidency, Zelenskyy oversaw some limited progress in tackling [corruption in Ukraine](33085862.md),[^cite_note-6][^cite_note-Wilson_2021-7][^cite_note-ft_profile-8] the ending of legal immunity for members of parliament (the [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md)),[^cite_note-9] as well as the country's [response to the COVID-19 pandemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Ukraine) and [subsequent economic recession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/COVID-19_recession).

During his early presidency, Zelenskyy [attempted to end](#Attempts_to_end_the_Donbas_conflict) the war in Donbas by implementing the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements) through the "[Steinmeier formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steinmeier_formula)", and sought dialogue with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md).[^cite_note-BBC_10_Dec_2019-10][^cite_note-Chatham_Lutsevych-11] During 2021, there was a large Russian military buildup on Ukraine's borders and increased attacks by [Russian proxy forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Ukraine), sparking [an international crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine). Zelenskyy said his military would not respond to provocations.[^cite_note-Guardian_18_Feb_2022-12] He attempted to calm the Ukrainian people and asked for military support from [NATO](21133.md).[^cite_note-Fr24_Seibt-13] On 24 February 2022, Russia launched a full-scale invasion of Ukraine. Zelenskyy remained in the capital [Kyiv](585629.md) as it came under attack, declining international offers to evacuate him. He declared [martial law across Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Martial_law_in_Ukraine) and a general mobilisation of the armed forces.[^cite_note-14][^cite_note-15] There have reportedly been numerous [Russian assassination attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_attempts_on_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy) on Zelenskyy since the invasion.

During the [early peace negotiations](70255183.md#Early_negotiations_\(February%E2%80%93April_2022\)), Zelenskyy demanded a ceasefire and Russian withdrawal, said he was willing to discuss Ukraine becoming a [neutral country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country) if given "real" security guarantees, and was willing to negotiate the status of Crimea and Donbas. He presented [Ukraine's Peace Formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_Peace_Formula) in late 2022, and the [Victory Plan for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Plan_for_Ukraine) in late 2024. After Russia ["annexed" southeastern Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts), Zelenskyy applied for [Ukraine to join NATO](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations). He has frequently visited frontline and newly liberated areas,[^cite_note-BBC_Bakhmut-16][^cite_note-BBC_Izyum-17][^cite_note-BBC_Kherson-18][^cite_note-KI_Kupiansk-19][^cite_note-AP_frontline-20][^cite_note-UP_Pokrovsk-21] and met with other world leaders, calling on the international community to support Ukraine's resistance, to provide Ukraine with [military aid](70175610.md), and to impose tougher sanctions on Russia to bring an end to its war. As well as visiting Ukrainian troops on the frontlines, Zelenskyy has repeatedly met with other world leaders, calling on the international community to support Ukraine's resistance and its efforts to end Russia's war. Zelenskyy has [called for an unconditional ceasefire](#Calls_for_ceasefire_and_peace_talks) and negotiations based on a [20-point peace plan](70255183.md).

Zelenskyy was named the [*Time* Person of the Year](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Time_Person_of_the_Year) for 2022.[^cite_note-Liga2020-22][^cite_note-slovoidilo-23][^cite_note-up_kuchma-24][^cite_note-25] Rating Group polls in 2022 and 2026 have ranked Zelenskyy among the five most outstanding Ukrainians of all time.[^cite_note-rating_outstanding_2026-26][^cite_note-prm_outstanding_2026-27][^cite_note-komersant_outstanding_2026-28][^cite_note-rating_outstanding-29][^cite_note-up_outstanding-30][^cite_note-lb_outstanding-31] Zelenskyy's term was originally scheduled to end in May 2024, but the ongoing Russian invasion and the resulting martial law prevents [the regularly scheduled presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Next_Ukrainian_presidential_election) from being held. He is expected to remain president for the duration of the war.[^cite_note-32] Due to this extension of his term, Zelenskyy is the [second-longest serving president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Ukrainian_presidents_by_length_of_tenure) after Kuchma.

## Early life

Volodymyr Oleksandrovych Zelenskyy was born into a [Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Ukraine) family on 25 January 1978 in [Kryvyi Rih](624764.md), an industrial city in [Ukrainian SSR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_SSR), Soviet Union.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-34][^cite_note-nyt1-35] His mother, Rymma Zelenska, is a retired engineer, while his father, [Oleksandr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Zelenskyy) – a professor and [computer scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Computer_scientist) – is the head of the Department of Cybernetics and Computing Hardware at the [Kryvyi Rih State University of Economics and Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryvyi_Rih_State_University_of_Economics_and_Technology).[^cite_note-36][^cite_note-37][^cite_note-gordon-38] Oleksandr's father, [Semyon Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semyon_Zelenskyy), served as an [infantryman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infantryman), reaching the rank of [colonel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polkovnik) in the [Red Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Army) (in the [57th Guards Motor Rifle Division](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/57th_Guards_Motor_Rifle_Division)) during the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_World_War).[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-DetroitJewishNews_20220228-40][^cite_note-41] Semyon's father and three brothers were killed during [the Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust_in_Ukraine).[^cite_note-18Things-42][^cite_note-43][^cite_note-44][^cite_note-afrhometown-45] Zelenskyy said in March 2022 that, during a massacre, both of Semyon's parents were killed after [German troops](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wehrmacht) burned their home to the ground.[^cite_note-46] Oleksandr's mother survived the Second World War after leaving Kryvyi Rih in an evacuation of Jews to [Almaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almaty), Kazakh SSR, and returned to Ukrainian SSR after the war.[^cite_note-timesofisrael-47]

Before starting [elementary school](5231751.md), Zelenskyy spent four years in [Erdenet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erdenet), Mongolia, where Oleksandr worked as a [mining engineer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mining_engineer) from the mid-1970s to help build a [copper mine](828834.md), applying his abilities in computer science to mining.[^cite_note-48] Zelenskyy grew up speaking [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language_in_Ukraine).[^cite_note-DetroitJewishNews_20220228-40][^cite_note-49] At the age of 16, he took the [Test of English as a Foreign Language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Test_of_English_as_a_Foreign_Language) and received an education grant to study in Israel, but Oleksandr did not allow him to go.[^cite_note-ZQ-50] Zelenskyy later earned a law degree from the [Kryvyi Rih Institute of Economics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryvyi_Rih_Institute_of_Economics), then a department of [Kyiv National Economic University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_National_Economic_University) and now part of [Kryvyi Rih National University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kryvyi_Rih_National_University), but never worked in the legal field.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-0396374-zelenskiy-51]

## Entertainment career

### *KVN* (1995–2003)

At age 17, Zelenskyy joined his local team competing in *[KVN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KVN)*,[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33] a comedy competition popular across [former Soviet countries](36870.md).[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 15–16 </sup> He was soon invited to join another Ukrainian team known as "Zaporizhzhia-Kryvyi Rih-Transit",[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-kvart-53] where he became dance director and writer in 1995.[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 60 </sup> The Transit team performed in KVN's Major League and eventually won in 1997[^cite_note-kvnru-55] in a tie with Armenian opponents.[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 184 </sup>

That same year, Zelenskyy created and headed a new team known as Kvartal 95 together with other Transit members, such as [Oleksandr Pikalov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Pikalov) and [Olena Kravets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Kravets);[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 60 </sup> this team was named after the [Kvartal 95 neighbourhood in Kryvyi Rih](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/95th_Kvartal,_Kryvyi_Rih) where he had grown up. From 1999 to 2003, Kvartal 95 performed in the highest open Ukrainian league of KVN as well as in the KVN Major League;[^cite_note-kvart-53] they were the only Ukrainian team in the Moscow-based highest league of KVN.[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 61 </sup> The team members lived in Moscow much of the time, without a fixed address and struggling financially;[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 20–21 </sup> they constantly toured former Soviet countries.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-kvart-53]



Zelenskyy in Prague in 2009

Zelenskyy and Kvartal 95 left KVN in 2003, after prolonged tension with KVN's Russian management over money, political censorship,[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 34 </sup> and other disputes.[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 214 </sup> Finally, Zelenskyy declined a highly paid job offer from KVN management that would have required him to abandon Kvartal 95, choosing instead to stay with his team and return to Ukraine.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-arte-57][^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 50 </sup>[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 107 </sup>[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 214 </sup> An incident where a Russian KVN producer used an antisemitic insult against Zelenskyy on stage also contributed to the split.[^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 50 </sup>[^cite_note-arte-57]<sup>: 45\:54 </sup>

### Kvartal 95 Studio (2003–2019)



Zelenskyy (left) and [Yevhen Koshovyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yevhen_Koshovyi) (right) co-hosting the opening portion of [Teletriumph 2011](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teletriumph_Awards), the national television awards of Ukraine. [Kvartal 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kvartal_95_Studio) wrote the script and hosted the 2011 ceremony.



Zelenskyy (centre) performing with Kvartal 95 in 2018

After leaving KVN, the Kvartal 95 team moved to [Kyiv](585629.md),[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 214 </sup> where Zelenskyy and the brothers Borys and [Serhiy Shefir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Shefir) – who had been members of the Transit KVN team – founded the [Kvartal 95 Studio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kvartal_95_Studio) company in September 2003.[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 36–37 </sup> That same year, Kvartal 95 started producing TV shows for the Ukrainian TV channel [1+1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1+1_\(TV_channel\)). In 2005, Kvartal 95 moved to the Ukrainian TV channel [Inter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter_\(TV_channel\)),[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 43 </sup> where they launched the *[Evening Kvartal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evening_Kvartal?action=edit\&redlink=1)* comedy show the same year.[^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 54 </sup>[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 42 </sup>[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 248 </sup> This show's format was "theatre-style" with a mixture of [skits](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skit_comedy), appearances by celebrities, and [stand-up comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand-up_comedy);[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 42–43 </sup> it included sketches on everyday life as well as political satire.[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 42–47 </sup>[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 136–138 </sup> *Evening Kvartal* was the most popular comedy television show in Ukraine in the 2000s,[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 248 </sup> and over 85% of Ukrainians reported they had seen it by the time Zelenskyy became president in 2019.[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 137 </sup> Kvartal 95 returned to 1+1 in 2012.[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 110 </sup>

Kvartal 95 has been described by biographers as an "empire", a "comedy factory",[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 51 </sup> and the "most successful production studio" in Ukraine.[^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 59 </sup> Apart from *Evening Kvartal*, the company also launched other show formats including Zelenskyy, such as [Chisto News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chisto_News?action=edit\&redlink=1), [League of Laughter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/League_of_Laughter?action=edit\&redlink=1), and [Make the Comedian Laugh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Make_the_Comedian_Laugh?action=edit\&redlink=1). By 2013, the third – a format in which contestants attempted to make comedians laugh – had been sold into China, Italy, and Finland,[^cite_note-comedianlaugh-59][^cite_note-detector_laugh-60] and Zelenskyy said in 2018 that he received royalties from 21 countries for the show.[^cite_note-ZQ-50]

As Zelenskyy's and Kvartal 95's popularity grew, he and they were frequently invited to host or perform at corporate and private events.[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 270 </sup> Zelenskyy said in 2018 that he had performed in front of numerous presidents of post-Soviet countries,[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 109 </sup> including his own predecessors in office, as well as then-Russian president [Dmitry Medvedev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dmitry_Medvedev).[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 270 </sup>[^cite_note-ZQ-50]

In 2006, Zelenskyy won the first season of [Ukraine's *Dancing with the Stars*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dancing_with_the_Stars_\(Ukraine\)).[^cite_note-Zygar-56]<sup>: 261 </sup>

Apart from comedy, Zelenskyy's film and television career included acting, scriptwriting, direction, and production. In 2008, he began to co-produce the [sitcom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sitcom) *[Svaty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Svaty)* ("The In-Laws"), which was highly popular in Ukraine and Russia[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 81 </sup> and was nominated for the Most Popular Sitcom award at the [Festival de Television de Monte-Carlo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Festival_de_Television_de_Monte-Carlo).[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 50 </sup> The same year, he starred in the romantic comedy *[Love in the Big City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_in_the_Big_City_\(2009_film\))*,[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33] and subsequently in its [2010](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_in_the_Big_City_2) and [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Love_in_the_Big_City_3) sequels; he was also co-writer of the second movie[^cite_note-bigcity2-61] and co-producer of the third.[^cite_note-bigcity3-62] In 2011, he co-wrote, co-produced, and played the male protagonist in the film *[Office Romance. Our Time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_Romance._Our_Time)*.[^cite_note-officeromance-63] In 2012, he co-produced *[Rzhevsky Versus Napoleon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rzhevsky_Versus_Napoleon)*[^cite_note-napoleon-64] and played the role of Napoleon.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33] Zelenskyy also co-wrote, co-produced, and played the male lead in the 2012 romantic comedy *[8 First Dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_First_Dates)* and its sequels *[8 Best Dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_Best_Dates)* (2012) and *[8 New Dates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_New_Dates)* (2014).[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33][^cite_note-8firstdates-65][^cite_note-8bestdates-66][^cite_note-8newdates-67] He also co-wrote, co-produced, and co-directed the 2018 romantic comedy *[Me. You. He. She](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me._You._He._She)*, and played the male protagonist.[^cite_note-youheshe-68]

Zelenskyy also recorded the voice of [Paddington Bear](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddington_Bear) in the Ukrainian [dub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubbing) of *[Paddington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddington_\(film\))* (2014) and *[Paddington 2](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paddington_2)* (2017), and the voice of Red in the Ukrainian dub of *[The Angry Birds Movie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Angry_Birds_Movie)*.[^cite_note-suspilne_dubs-69]

Zelenskyy was also popular in Russia before 2014.[^cite_note-lemonde_russia-70][^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 51 </sup> In 2011, he presented [Russia's *X Factor*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Faktor_A) with [Filipp Kirkorov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipp_Kirkorov) and [Alla Pugacheva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alla_Pugacheva).[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 51 </sup> In 2013, he presented the New Year's Eve show on the [Russia-1](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russia-1) channel with Russian comedian [Maxim Galkin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maxim_Galkin).[^cite_note-lemonde_russia-70] After Russia's [2014 annexation of Crimea](42228673.md), Kvartal 95 closed its office in Moscow and began shutting down business ties with Russia; Zelenskyy himself stopped working in Russia by the end of 2014.[^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 106–110 </sup> By Zelenskyy's estimation, the decision to separate from the Russian market reduced Kvartal 95's average revenue per hour of TV programming from \$200,000 to \$30,000.[^cite_note-ZQ-50][^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 106–110 </sup>



Zelenskyy (centre) with Kvartal 95 visiting wounded Ukrainian soldiers in [Odesa](55545.md) in 2016

From 2014, Kvartal 95 also began visiting the war zone to perform for Ukrainian soldiers at the front in the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas).[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 70–71 </sup>[^cite_note-showman-58]<sup>: 106–110 </sup>[^cite_note-arte-57][^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 246 </sup> After Ukrainian media reported that Kvartal 95 had donated [₴](722696.md)1 million to the [Ukrainian army](7433327.md) after the start of the [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md), some Russian politicians and artists petitioned for a ban on Zelenskyy's works in Russia.[^cite_note-Zelensky1509516953-71][^cite_note-72]

In August 2014, Zelenskyy spoke out against the intention of the [Ukrainian Ministry of Culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Culture_\(Ukraine\)) to ban Russian artists from Ukraine.[^cite_note-sevas.com358742-73] Since 2015, Ukraine has blacklisted some Russian artists and media deemed part of "information warfare", such as for "anti-Ukrainian rhetoric" or supporting the annexation of Crimea, and barred them from entering Ukraine.[^cite_note-Zelensky33863697-74] In 2018, Zelenskyy's *Love in the Big City 2* was banned in Ukraine[^cite_note-75] as it included a blacklisted Russian actor.[^cite_note-bigcity2banned-76] Similarly, the sitcom *Svaty* co-produced by Zelenskyy was banned in Ukraine in 2017,[^cite_note-77] after a Russian lead actor was blacklisted due to allegedly visiting occupied Crimea and supporting the annexation;[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 81 </sup> however, it was unbanned in March 2019.[^cite_note-78]

In 2015, Zelenskyy began to co-produce and star in the television series *[Servant of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People_\(2015_TV_series\))*, where he played the role of the president of Ukraine.[^cite_note-0396374-zelenskiy-51][^cite_note-sotp-79] In the series, Zelenskyy's character was a high-school history teacher in his 30s who won the presidential election after a [viral video](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viral_video) showed him ranting against government [corruption in Ukraine](33085862.md). The show ran for three seasons until Zelenskyy's own election as president.

In 2016, while he was the artistic director of Kvartal 95, Zelenskyy co-presented the [Teletriumph Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teletriumph_Awards), the national television awards of Ukraine, alongside [Masha Efrosynina](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masha_Efrosynina).[^cite_note-\:9-80][^cite_note-\:11-81] Teletriumph 2016 was held at [Freedom Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Hall_\(Kyiv\)?action=edit\&redlink=1) and was not televised.[^cite_note-\:11-81][^cite_note-\:9-80] He also had to leave the stage to accept his own award that night.[^cite_note-\:9-80] However, the television channel that Kvartal 95 had been associated with, Inter, and their parent company Inter Media Group, boycotted the event and did not participate in the nomination process due to disputes within the Ukrainian television industry and the awards organisers.[^cite_note-\:9-80] Zelenskyy reportedly delivered a biting punchline about the boycott.[^cite_note-\:9-80] The president of Inter Media Group then circulated a press release in response.[^cite_note-82]

Zelenskyy worked mostly in Russian-language productions. The romantic comedy *[Me. You. He. She](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Me._You._He._She)*, released in Ukraine in December 2018, was his first acting role in the Ukrainian language.[^cite_note-83][^cite_note-84] The first version of the script was written in Ukrainian but was translated into Russian for the Lithuanian actress [Agnė Grudytė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agn%C4%97_Grudyt%C4%97). Later, the movie was [dubbed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dubbing_\(filmmaking\)#Ukraine) into Ukrainian.[^cite_note-85]

### Inter general producer (2010–2012)

Zelenskyy was a member of the board and the general producer of the TV channel Inter from 2010 to 2012,[^cite_note-0396374-zelenskiy-51] where he created 30 new formats.[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 51 </sup> Later in 2018, Zelenskyy said that [then-President Yanukovych](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Yanukovych) had offered him US\$100 million for political control of Kvartal 95's programmes after he became general producer of Inter, but Zelenskyy refused.[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 54 </sup>[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 109–110 </sup>[^cite_note-ZQ-50]

## 2019 presidential election

Main article: [2019 Ukrainian presidential election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_presidential_election)



Zelenskyy and then-president of Ukraine [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko), April 2019

In March 2018, members of Zelenskyy's production company [Kvartal 95](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kvartal_95_Studio) registered a new political party called *[Servant of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People_\(political_party\))* – the same name as the television programme in which Zelenskyy had played the President of Ukraine for the previous three years.[^cite_note-SPppno31217bb-86][^cite_note-up7196270Z-87] Although Zelenskyy denied any immediate plans to enter politics and said he had registered the party name only to prevent it being appropriated by others,[^cite_note-\:6-88] there was widespread speculation that he was planning to run. As early as October 2018, three months before his campaign announcement and six months before the presidential election, he was already a frontrunner in opinion polls.[^cite_note-89][^cite_note-up7196270Z-87] After months of ambiguous statements,[^cite_note-\:6-88][^cite_note-up7196270Z-87] on 31 December, less than four months from the election, Zelenskyy announced his candidacy for president of Ukraine on the New Year's Eve evening show on the TV channel 1+1.[^cite_note-ReutersScrutiny-90] His announcement aired during the traditional New Year's Eve address of incumbent president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) on the same channel,[^cite_note-ReutersScrutiny-90] which was only played after Zelenskyy's announcement; Zelenskyy said this was unintentional and attributed it to a technical glitch.[^cite_note-91]

Zelenskyy's presidential campaign against Poroshenko was almost entirely virtual.[^cite_note-zelensky-190416154745843-92][^cite_note-93] He did not release a detailed policy platform[^cite_note-47654242_UkraineKvartal_95-94] and his engagement with mainstream media was minimal;[^cite_note-zelensky-190416154745843-92][^cite_note-96] he instead reached out to the electorate via social media channels and [YouTube](3524766.md) clips.[^cite_note-zelensky-190416154745843-92] In place of traditional campaign rallies, he conducted [stand-up comedy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stand-up_comedy) routines across Ukraine with his production company Kvartal 95.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] On 16 April 2019, a few days before the second round of the election, 20 Ukrainian news outlets called on Zelenskyy to "stop avoiding journalists".[^cite_note-zelensky-190416154745843-92] Zelenskyy stated that he was not hiding from journalists but that he did not want to go to talk shows where "people of the old power" were "just doing [PR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_relations)" and that he did not have time to satisfy all interview requests.[^cite_note-99]

The third season of *[Servant of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People_\(2015_TV_series\))* started and ended during the 2019 campaign.[^cite_note-ZelenskyEffect-54]<sup>: 160 </sup>

Zelenskyy styled himself as an [anti-establishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-establishment), anti-corruption figure, and said he wished to restore trust in politicians, "to bring professional, decent people to power" and to "change the mood and timbre of the political establishment".[^cite_note-SPppno31217bb-86][^cite_note-up7196270Z-87][^cite_note-lection2019555634-100] [Atlantic Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atlantic_Council) member [Anders Aslund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anders_Aslund) said Zelenskyy was not a [populist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Populism);[^cite_note-47654242_UkraineKvartal_95-94] however, other scholars have analysed Zelenskyy's politics in the framework of populism. For instance, political scientist Volodymyr Kulyk described Zelenskyy as practising "inclusionary" populism that claims to defend "the people" against "the elite" while defining "the people" in a way inclusive of all ethnic, linguistic, religious, and other groups.[^cite_note-Kulyk-101] Scholar Kostiantyn Yanchenko described Zelenskyy's campaign as characterised by "populist [hyperreality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyperreality)" constructed by the Servant of the People series and Zelenskyy's other comedy shows,[^cite_note-hyperreality-102] and argued along with fellow scholar Mattia Zulianello that Zelenskyy and his party were examples of "valence populism" focused on "non-positional topics, such as anti-corruption appeals, political transparency and moral integrity", rather than "economic and socio-cultural issues".[^cite_note-valence-103]

Before the elections, Zelenskyy presented a team that included former finance minister [Oleksandr Danylyuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Danylyuk) and others.[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-7212658/_Zelensky-95] During the campaign, concerns were raised over his links to the oligarch [Ihor Kolomoyskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ihor_Kolomoyskyi),[^cite_note-105] a billionaire businessman who had gained control of the [1+1 Media Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1+1_Media_Group) in 2010. The group operates eight Ukrainian TV channels and broadcast *Servant of the People* from 2015 to 2019.

Poroshenko and his supporters argued that Zelenskyy's victory would benefit Russia.[^cite_note-106][^cite_note-107][^cite_note-108][^cite_note-slate-109] On 19 April 2019 at [Olimpiyskiy National Sports Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olimpiyskiy_National_Sports_Complex) presidential debates were held in the form of a show.[^cite_note-hroma190419-110][^cite_note-111][^cite_note-112] In his introductory speech, Zelenskyy acknowledged that in 2014 he voted for Poroshenko, but "I was mistaken. We were mistaken. We voted for one Poroshenko, but received another. The first appears when there are video cameras, the other Petro sends [Medvedchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Medvedchuk) privietiki (greetings) to Moscow."[^cite_note-hroma190419-110] Although Zelenskyy initially said he would serve only a single term, he walked back this promise in May 2021, saying he had not yet made up his mind.[^cite_note-7294216Zelensky-113]

Zelenskyy stated that as president he would develop the [economy](31829.md) and attract investment to Ukraine through "a restart of the [judicial system](1238343.md)" and restoring confidence in the state.[^cite_note-7210004/_Zelensky-114] He also proposed a tax amnesty and a 5-per-cent [flat tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flat_tax) for big business which could be increased "in dialogue with them and if everyone agrees".[^cite_note-7210004/_Zelensky-114] According to Zelenskyy, if people would notice that his new government "works honestly from the first day", they would start paying their taxes.[^cite_note-7210004/_Zelensky-114]

Zelenskyy achieved a plurality of the electorate (30%) in the first round of elections on 31 March 2019.[^cite_note-CVKResults1stRound-115] In the second round, on 21 April 2019, he received 73 percent of the vote to Poroshenko's 25 percent in the biggest landslide victory in Ukrainian presidential elections[^cite_note-slovoidilo-23] and was elected President of Ukraine.[^cite_note-116][^cite_note-117] Polish president [Andrzej Duda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Duda) was one of the first European leaders to congratulate Zelenskyy.[^cite_note-118] French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) received Zelenskyy at the [Élysée Palace](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C3%89lys%C3%A9e_Palace) in Paris on 12 April 2019.[^cite_note-119] On 22 April, U.S. president [Donald Trump](4848272.md) congratulated Zelenskyy on his victory over the telephone.[^cite_note-120][^cite_note-121] [European Commission](9974.md) president [Jean Claude Juncker](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jean_Claude_Juncker) and [European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Council) president [Donald Tusk](648724.md) also issued a joint letter of congratulations and stated that the [European Union](9317.md) (EU) will work to speed up the implementation of the remainder of the [EU–Ukraine Association Agreement](39410190.md), including the [Deep and Comprehensive Free Trade Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deep_and_Comprehensive_Free_Trade_Area).[^cite_note-122]

### Before the 2022 Russian invasion

#### Inauguration

Main article: [Inauguration of Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inauguration_of_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy)



Zelenskyy during his inauguration

Zelenskyy was inaugurated on 20 May 2019.[^cite_note-123] Various foreign officials attended the ceremony in Ukraine's parliament ([Verkhovna Rada](844993.md)), including [Salome Zourabichvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salome_Zourabichvili) ([Georgia](48768.md)), [Kersti Kaljulaid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kersti_Kaljulaid) ([Estonia](28222445.md)), [Raimonds Vējonis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raimonds_V%C4%93jonis) ([Latvia](17514.md)), [Dalia Grybauskaitė](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dalia_Grybauskait%C4%97) ([Lithuania](17675.md)), [János Áder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_%C3%81der) ([Hungary](13275.md)), [Maroš Šefčovič](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maro%C5%A1_%C5%A0ef%C4%8Dovi%C4%8D) (EU), and [Rick Perry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rick_Perry) (United States).[^cite_note-124] Zelenskyy is the first Jewish president of Ukraine; with [Volodymyr Groysman](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Groysman) as prime minister, Ukraine became the first country other than [Israel](9282173.md) to simultaneously have a Jewish head of state and head of government.[^cite_note-nyt1-35]

In his inaugural address, Zelenskyy dissolved the [Ukrainian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8th_Ukrainian_Verkhovna_Rada) and called for [early parliamentary elections](462915.md) (which had originally been scheduled for October of that year).[^cite_note-125] One of the parliamentary coalition members, the [People's Front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Front_\(Ukraine\)), opposed the move and withdrew from the ruling coalition shortly before Zelenskyy's inauguration.[^cite_note-126]

#### Early presidency

On 28 May 2019, Zelenskyy restored the Ukrainian citizenship of [Mikheil Saakashvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mikheil_Saakashvili).[^cite_note-127]



Zelenskyy at a meeting with the Director of the [National Anti-Corruption Bureau of Ukraine](33096574.md) (NABU), [Artem Sytnyk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artem_Sytnyk), and the Head of the [Specialized Anti-Corruption Prosecutor's Office](57303521.md) (SAPO) [Nazar Kholodnytskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nazar_Kholodnytskyi), May 2019

Zelenskyy's first major proposal to change the electoral system from a [plurality voting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Plurality_voting) system to [proportional representation](23298.md) with [closed party lists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Closed_list) was strongly rejected by the [Ukrainian parliament](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_parliament), who believed that closed lists would lead to more corruption in government.[^cite_note-uawireclosedlists-128] Zelenskyy had earlier called for the introduction of [open list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_list) election ballots, such as during his inauguration speech,[^cite_note-inaugurationspeech-129] but his proposal would have held the [2019 Ukrainian parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election) with closed lists based on the reasoning that the 60-day term to the snap election did not "leave any chances for the introduction of this system."[^cite_note-UPclosedlistsrejected-130]

In addition, on 6 June, lawmakers refused to include Zelenskyy's key initiative on reintroducing criminal liability for illegal enrichment in the parliament's agenda, and instead included a similar bill proposed by a group of deputies.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132] In June 2019 it was announced that the president's third major initiative, which seeks to remove immunity from lawmakers, diplomats and judges, would be submitted after the July [2019 Ukrainian parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election).[^cite_note-133] This initiative was completed on 3 September, when the new parliament passed a bill stripping lawmakers of legal immunity, delivering Zelenskyy a legislative victory by fulfilling one of his key campaign promises.[^cite_note-134]

On 8 July, Zelenskyy ordered the cancellation of the annual [Kyiv Independence Day Parade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_Independence_Day_Parade) on [Maidan Nezalezhnosti](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maidan_Nezalezhnosti), citing costs. Despite this, Zelenskyy highlighted that [the day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_of_Ukraine#2019) would "honor heroes" on Independence Day, however, the "format will be new."[^cite_note-135][^cite_note-136] He also proposed to spend the money that would have been used to finance the parade on veterans.[^cite_note-137]



Zelenskyy at the 14-hour press conference in a Kyiv food court, 10 October 2019

On 10 October 2019, Zelenskyy held a 14-hour press conference in a Kyiv food court, where he took questions from about 300 Ukrainian and foreign journalists.[^cite_note-atlantic_14h-138][^cite_note-guardian_14h-139][^cite_note-euronews_14h-140][^cite_note-ukrinform_14h-141] Journalists attended in groups of seven to ten which switched every hour, with short breaks of up to ten minutes between groups.[^cite_note-ukrinform_14h-141] Zelenskyy answered questions about the [first impeachment of Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_impeachment_of_Donald_Trump); issues related to the [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md), such as [Crimea](163045.md), the [Steinmeier formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steinmeier_formula), troop disengagement in Donbas, prisoner exchanges and the [Normandy Format](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Format); his relationship with oligarch [Ihor Kolomoyskyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ihor_Kolomoyskyi); and other questions.[^cite_note-guardian_14h-139][^cite_note-atlantic_14h-138][^cite_note-ukrinform_14h-141] The length of the press conference was announced to be a world record[^cite_note-ukrinform_14h-141][^cite_note-guardian_14h-139][^cite_note-euronews_14h-140][^cite_note-derix-142]<sup>: 105 </sup> until the record was broken by [Maldives](19117.md) president [Mohamed Muizzu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mohamed_Muizzu) in May 2025.[^cite_note-unn_maldives-143][^cite_note-st_maldives-144]

In 2020, Zelenskyy's party proposed reforms to Ukraine's media laws with the intent to increase competition and loosen the dominance of [Ukrainian oligarchs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_oligarchs) on television and radio broadcasters. Critics said it risked increasing [media censorship in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_of_the_press_in_Ukraine)[^cite_note-145] because its clause of criminal responsibility for the distribution of disinformation could be abused.[^cite_note-146]

In January 2020, Zelenskyy took a trip to [Oman](22316.md) that was not published on his official schedule, appearing to combine a personal holiday with government business. His office said Zelenskyy paid for the entire trip himself. Nevertheless, he was criticised for a lack of transparency and critics pointed out he had once criticised his predecessor Poroshenko for taking an undisclosed vacation in the [Maldives](19117.md).[^cite_note-pravdaua200105-147][^cite_note-radiolib200106-148]

In his 4 March 2020 address to the Rada, Zelenskyy recommitted to reforms domestic and financial, and remarked that he "cannot always become a psychologist for people, a crisis manager for someone, a collector who requires honestly earned money, and a nanny of the ministry in charge."[^cite_note-pukz-149]

By September 2020, Zelenskyy's approval ratings had fallen to less than 32 percent.[^cite_note-150]

In January 2021, parliament passed a bill updating and reforming Ukraine's [referendum](51582.md) laws,[^cite_note-\:4-151] which Ukraine's [Constitutional Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Ukraine) had declared unconstitutional in 2018.[^cite_note-152] Fixing the referendum law had been one of Zelenskyy's campaign promises.[^cite_note-\:4-151]

On 24 March 2021, Zelenskyy signed Decree 117/2021 approving the "strategy for de-occupation and reintegration of the temporarily occupied territory of the [Autonomous Republic of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Autonomous_Republic_of_Crimea) and the city of [Sevastopol](51587.md)."[^cite_note-153]

In June 2021, Zelenskyy submitted to the Verkhovna Rada [a bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_on_Oligarchs_\(Ukraine\)) creating a public registry of Ukraine's oligarchs, banning them from participating in privatisations of [state-owned companies](204768.md) and forbidding them from contributing financially to politicians. Opposition party leaders supported Zelenskyy's goal of reducing oligarchs' influence on politics in Ukraine but were critical of his approach, saying the public register would be both dangerous, as it concentrated power in the president; and ineffective, since oligarchs were merely a "symbol" of more deeply-rooted corruption.[^cite_note-154] The bill was passed into law in September 2021.[^cite_note-155] Critics of Zelenskyy's administration have claimed that, in taking power away from the Ukrainian oligarchs, he has sought to centralise authority and strengthen his position.[^cite_note-156]

By October 2021, Zelenskyy's approval rating had fallen further to 24.7%, but was still above or on par with most of his predecessors' at the same point in their presidencies.[^cite_note-slovoidilo-23] That month, the [Pandora Papers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pandora_Papers) revealed that Zelenskyy, his chief aide, and the head of the [Security Service of Ukraine](709222.md) [Ivan Bakanov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ivan_Bakanov) operated a network of offshore companies in the [British Virgin Islands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Virgin_Islands), [Cyprus](5593.md), and [Belize](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Belize). These companies included some that owned expensive London property.[^cite_note-PandoraZelensky-157] Around the time of his [2019 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_presidential_election), Zelenskyy handed his shares in a key offshore company over to [Serhiy Shefir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Shefir), but the two men appear to have arranged for Zelenskyy's family to continue receiving the money from these companies.[^cite_note-PandoraZelensky-157] Zelenskyy's election campaign had centred on pledges to clean up the [government of Ukraine](1372585.md).[^cite_note-PandoraZelensky-157] Responding to the controversy, Zelenskyy said that the offshore companies had been set up to protect Kvartal 95 Studio from political pressure under the Yanukovych administration, and said this had been common practice during Yanukovych's presidency, especially in the media industry.[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 133 </sup>[^cite_note-dw_pandora-158] The same month, the [National Agency on Corruption Prevention](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Agency_on_Corruption_Prevention) announced that they had found no signs of conflicts of interest, illicit enrichment, or unjustified assets after investigating Zelenskyy's businesses,[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 133 </sup>[^cite_note-radiosvoboda_pandora-159][^cite_note-thepage_pandora-160] and that Zelenskyy had provided letters stating that payments had not been made through the relevant offshore companies to his wife [Olena Zelenska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zelenska) in 2019 or 2020.[^cite_note-delo_pandora-161][^cite_note-detector_pandora-162][^cite_note-nv_pandora-163][^cite_note-up_pandora-164][^cite_note-kp_pandora-165][^cite_note-thepage_pandora-160]

#### Cabinets and administration



Zelenskyy and Shmyhal in 2019

Zelenskyy appointed [Andriy Bohdan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriy_Bohdan) as head of the [Presidential Administration of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Presidential_Administration_of_Ukraine). Before this, Bohdan had been the lawyer of Ukrainian oligarch Ihor Kolomoyskyi.[^cite_note-auto2-166] Under the rules of [lustration in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lustration_in_Ukraine), introduced in 2014 following [Euromaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan), Bohdan is not entitled to hold any state office until 2024 (because of his government post during the [Second Azarov Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Azarov_Government)).[^cite_note-auto2-166] Bohdan, however, contended that because heading the presidential administration is not considered civil service work, lustration did not apply to him.[^cite_note-167] A number of the members of the Presidential Administration Zelenskyy appointed were former colleagues from his former production company, Kvartal 95,[^cite_note-auto2-166] including Ivan Bakanov, who became deputy head of the Ukrainian Secret Service (SBU).[^cite_note-168] Former deputy foreign minister [Olena Zerkal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zerkal) declined an appointment as deputy head of the presidential administration, but did agree to serve as the Ukrainian representative of the international courts concerning Russia.[^cite_note-169] Zelenskyy's requests to replace the foreign minister, [defence minister](9473138.md), chief prosecutor and head of Ukraine's security service were rejected by parliament.[^cite_note-170][^cite_note-171] Zelenskyy also dismissed and replaced 20 of the governors of Ukraine's 24 [oblasts](994124.md).[^cite_note-172]

Main article: [Honcharuk government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honcharuk_government)

In the 21 July 2019 parliamentary election, Zelenskyy's political party, Servant of the People, won the first single-party majority in modern Ukrainian history in parliament, with 43 percent of the party-list vote. His party gained 254 of the 424 seats.[^cite_note-173]

Following the elections, Zelenskyy nominated [Oleksiy Honcharuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksiy_Honcharuk) as prime minister, who was quickly confirmed by parliament. Parliament also confirmed [Andrii Zahorodniuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrii_Zahorodniuk) as defence minister, [Vadym Prystaiko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vadym_Prystaiko) as foreign minister and Bakanov as head of the SBU.[^cite_note-174] [Arsen Avakov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arsen_Avakov), a controversial figure dogged by longstanding corruption allegations,[^cite_note-175] was kept on as [interior minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interior_minister), with Honcharuk arguing that the relatively inexperienced government needed experienced administrators and that Avakov had been "'drawn red lines' that cannot be crossed."[^cite_note-176]

Zelenskyy dismissed Bohdan as head of his presidential administration on 11 February 2020 and appointed [Andriy Yermak](63086056.md) as his successor the same day.[^cite_note-640533YermakZelensky-177]

See also: [Land reforms by country § Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reforms_by_country#Ukraine)

In March 2020, Honchurak resigned as prime minister following the leak of an audio recording in which he appeared to belittle Zelenskyy's economic management. Honchurak was replaced as prime minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal).[^cite_note-178] Honchurak's hasty departure caused disquiet both in Ukraine and abroad, with many economists and political observers warning it would bring instability.[^cite_note-btkp-179]

#### Attempts to end the Donbas conflict



Zelenskyy visiting the frontline in the Donbas, May 2019

One of Zelenskyy's main campaign pledges had been to end the [War in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas) in eastern Ukraine, part of the [Russo-Ukrainian war](42085878.md).[^cite_note-\:3-180] The conflict had begun in 2014, when Russia [occupied](71944314.md) and [annexed Crimea](42228673.md), then supported [separatist paramilitaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Ukraine) who started a war in the eastern Donbas region, seized territory and declared themselves independent states. Russia covertly supported the separatists with its own troops, tanks and artillery, preventing Ukraine from fully retaking the territory. The [International Criminal Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Criminal_Court_investigation_in_Ukraine) (ICC) judged that the war was both a national and international armed conflict involving Russia,[^cite_note-181] and the [European Court of Human Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights) judged that Russia controlled the separatists from 2014 onward.[^cite_note-182]

On 3 June 2019, Zelenskyy appointed former president [Leonid Kuchma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Kuchma) as Ukraine's representative in the [Tripartite Contact Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trilateral_Contact_Group_on_Ukraine) for a settlement in the conflict.[^cite_note-183] On 11 July 2019, Zelenskyy held his first telephone conversation with Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md), during which he urged Putin to enter into talks mediated by EU.[^cite_note-184][^cite_note-\:1-185] The two leaders also discussed the exchange of prisoners held by both sides.[^cite_note-\:1-185]

A public opinion poll, held in separatist-controlled Donbas in March 2019 by the [Centre for East European and International Studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_East_European_and_International_Studies), found that 55% of those polled wanted reintegration with Ukraine.[^cite_note-186]

On 1 October 2019, Zelenskyy agreed to try to end the Donbas conflict by implementing parts of the [Minsk agreements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minsk_agreements) through the "[Steinmeier formula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steinmeier_formula)". Russia demanded this before any continuation of the "[Normandy Format](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Format)" peace talks. The plan foresaw holding elections, under Ukrainian law, in the territory held by Russian-backed separatists. The elections would be observed by the [Organization for Security and Cooperation in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Cooperation_in_Europe) (OSCE). Should the OSCE deem the elections free and fair, the separatist territories would be re-integrated into Ukraine and given permanent "special status", meaning they would be largely self-governing.[^cite_note-KyivPost_Oct_2019-187]



A "No to capitulation!" protest in Kyiv, October 2019

Zelenskyy faced strong opposition over the plan. Opponents argued that it would legalise the occupation of the Donbas and leave it as "a deadly [Trojan horse](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trojan_horse) under Russian control".[^cite_note-KyivPost_Oct_2019-187] Critics argued that elections held in Donbas were unlikely to be free and fair, that the separatists had long driven most pro-Ukrainian residents out of the region to ensure a pro-Russia majority, and that it would be impossible to ensure Russia kept its end of the agreement.[^cite_note-\:3-180] It was noted that the wording of the Minsk agreements is unclear on whether Russian troops would have to leave and if their proxy forces would have to disarm before elections.[^cite_note-KyivPost_Oct_2019-187]

There were protests against Zelenskyy and his government for agreeing to the plan, dubbed "[No to capitulation!](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/No_to_capitulation!)". About 10,000 protested in Kyiv, including Ukrainian far-right nationalists.[^cite_note-\:3-180][^cite_note-188] He was also criticised by Ukrainian opposition politicians. Former president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko) dismissed it as "Putin's formula", saying it would make the Donbas "an anchor blocking Ukraine's aspirations towards European and Euro-Atlantic aspirations".[^cite_note-KyivPost_Oct_2019-187] Several Ukrainian nationalist militias opposed the plan, including [Azov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azov_Battalion) fighters in the [Luhansk region](494904.md) of Donbas. Zelenskyy met personally with some of these groups and tried to convince them to accept the plan. [Andriy Biletsky](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriy_Biletsky), leader of the far-right [National Corps](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Corps) and first commander of Azov, accused Zelenskyy of being disrespectful to army veterans and of acting on behalf of [the Kremlin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Kremlin) by leaving Ukrainians vulnerable to Russian aggression.[^cite_note-189]

The leaders of the Donbas separatists, [Denis Pushilin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denis_Pushilin) and [Leonid Pasechnik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Pasechnik), stated: "It will be *we* who decide the language we speak, what kind of economy we have, how our judicial system will be formed, how our [people's militia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Ukraine) will be defending our citizens, and how we will be integrated into Russia. ... Kyiv authorities will not get any sort of control over the border with Russia".[^cite_note-KyivPost_Oct_2019-187]

Zelenskyy responded that he would not allow the elections to be held "at gunpoint". He said there must first be a ceasefire, a return of prisoners, withdrawal of Russian forces, and Ukrainian guards on the border. He also suggested that Ukrainians forced to flee the Donbas could be allowed to vote. Zelenskyy said that a ceasefire and Russian withdrawal would be negotiated at the next "Normandy format" meeting.[^cite_note-190]

As part of the plan, Ukrainian and pro-Russian forces agreed to pull back from three parts of the frontline: at [Stanytsia Luhanska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanytsia_Luhanska), [Zolote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zolote) and [Petrivske](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petrivka,_Volnovakha_Raion,_Donetsk_Oblast).[^cite_note-BBC_10_Dec_2019-10]



Zelenskyy with Macron and Putin during a [Normandy Format](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Format) meeting, December 2019

On 9 December 2019, Russia and Ukraine resumed talks in Paris, mediated by [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) of France and [Angela Merkel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angela_Merkel) of Germany under the so-called "Normandy format", which had been abandoned in 2016. It was Zelenskyy's first face-to-face meeting with Vladimir Putin.[^cite_note-191] Zelenskyy set out his conditions for elections in the Donbas:

- A full ceasefire and withdrawal of all armed groups

- Release of all Ukrainian prisoners

- Ukrainian control over the 400km (249-mile) stretch of the Russia-Ukraine border that rebels controlled

- Participation of Ukrainian parties and candidates, not just pro-Russian parties

- The right of all those who fled the conflict to return to vote

  [^cite_note-BBC_10_Dec_2019-10]

Zelenskyy and Putin pledged to work toward holding these elections, but disagreed over the timing and other issues. They agreed that a full ceasefire would begin by the end of the year and all remaining prisoners would be released. They also agreed to pull forces back from three more frontline areas by March 2020. However, Putin insisted that control of the border should only be dealt with after the elections.[^cite_note-BBC_10_Dec_2019-10] The Russian side continued to deny that its military was operating in the Donbas, instead calling them "Russian volunteers". Another summit was planned to be held in four months.[^cite_note-BBC_10_Dec_2019-10]

The talks failed to end the conflict, as the separatists continued their attacks and Russia continued providing them with weapons, ammunition and other support.[^cite_note-\:7-192]

On 27 July 2020, another ceasefire was agreed – the more than twentieth attempt since the war began in 2014. However, the OSCE reported more than 100 ceasefire violations within two days, without indicating which side had committed them.[^cite_note-193] Nevertheless, ceasefire violations halved in 2020 compared to the year before.[^cite_note-194]

The Ukrainian parliament announced that the [October 2020 local elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_Ukrainian_local_elections) could not be held in the Russian-occupied part of the Donbas, because the Minsk agreements called for them to be held under Ukrainian law. Russian president Putin insisted that elections be held there while Russian and Russian-backed forces still controlled the region and the border.[^cite_note-195]

#### UIA Flight 752

|  | This section **may lack focus** or be about [more than one topic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:REORGANIZE). Please help [improve this article](https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Volodymyr_Zelenskyy\&action=edit), possibly by [splitting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wikipedia\:Splitting) this section. Relevant discussion may be found on the [talk page](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Talk\:Volodymyr_Zelenskyy#). *(May 2026)*_&#x20;([Learn how and when to remove this message](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Maintenance_template_removal))_ |
| --- | --- |

See also: [Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_International_Airlines_Flight_752)



Zelenskyy meeting the coffins of Ukrainians killed in the Iranian shootdown of UIA Flight 752

On 8 January 2020, the Presidential Office announced that Volodymyr Zelenskyy was cutting short his trip to Oman owing to the [Ukraine International Airlines Flight 752](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine_International_Airlines_Flight_752) plane crash in nearby [Iran](14653.md) the same day.[^cite_note-196] Also on the same day, internet news site [Obozrevatel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obozrevatel).com released information that on 7 January 2020, Ukrainian politician of the [Opposition Platform — For Life](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_Platform_%E2%80%94_For_Life) Medvedchuk – who has exclusive relations with the current president of Russia – may have arrived in Oman.[^cite_note-Grigorenko_2020-197][^cite_note-198] Soon, rumours began that Zelenskyy may have had some additional meetings beside the ones that were announced.[^cite_note-199] On 14 January 2020, Yermak dismissed the rumours as speculations and baseless conspiracy theories,[^cite_note-200] while Medvedchuk stated that the plane was used by his older daughter's family to fly from Oman to Moscow.[^cite_note-201] Later, Yermak contacted the on-line newspaper *[Ukrainian Truth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainska_Pravda)* and gave more details about the visit to Oman and the plane crash in Iran.[^cite_note-202]

On 17 January 2020, the presidential appointee Minister of Foreign Affairs, Prystaiko, was unable to give answers during the "times of questions to the government" in parliament when the people's deputies of Ukraine asked him about the visit's official agenda, the invitation from Oman, officials of the Ministry of Foreign Affairs who were preparing the visit, as well as how the president actually crossed the border while visiting Oman.[^cite_note-203][^cite_note-204] On 20 January 2020, Prystaiko followed up by giving a briefing to the press in the Office of the president of Ukraine and saying that he would explain everything about the visit when the time came.[^cite_note-205]

#### Early foreign relations

See also: [List of international presidential trips made by Volodymyr Zelenskyy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_international_presidential_trips_made_by_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy)

  Zelenskyy at the Batumi International Conference with Georgian president [Salomé Zourabichvili](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salom%C3%A9_Zourabichvili), Moldovan president [Maia Sandu](40796149.md) and European Council president [Charles Michel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Michel), 19 July 2021

  Zelenskyy and US president Donald Trump, September 2019

  Zelenskyy and US president [Joe Biden](145422.md), September 2021

  Zelenskyy and Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) in October 2020

  Zelenskyy with [President of the European Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_European_Council), [Donald Tusk](648724.md), 2019
