  Zelenskyy with NATO Secretary-General [Jens Stoltenberg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jens_Stoltenberg), 2019

Zelenskyy's first official trip abroad as president was to Brussels in June 2019, where he met with EU and [NATO](21133.md) officials.[^cite_note-206]

In August 2019, Zelenskyy promised to lift the moratorium on exhuming Polish mass graves in Ukraine after the previous Ukrainian government banned the Polish side from carrying out any exhumations of Polish victims of the [Ukrainian Insurgent Army](1045217.md)-perpetrated [Volhynian massacres](464698.md), following the removal of a memorial to the Ukrainian Insurgent Army in [Hruszowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hruszowice), southeastern Poland.[^cite_note-207]

In September 2019, it was reported that U.S. president Trump had allegedly blocked payment of a congressionally mandated \$400-million military aid package to Ukraine to pressure Zelenskyy during a [July phone call](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_phone_call) between the two presidents to investigate alleged wrongdoing by Joe Biden and his son [Hunter Biden](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hunter_Biden),[^cite_note-slate-109][^cite_note-\:2-208] who took a board seat on Ukrainian natural gas company [Burisma Holdings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burisma_Holdings).[^cite_note-209] This report was the catalyst for the [Trump–Ukraine scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Trump%E2%80%93Ukraine_scandal) and the [impeachment inquiry against Donald Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impeachment_inquiry_against_Donald_Trump). Zelenskyy has denied that he was pressured by Trump and declared that "he does not want to interfere in a foreign election."[^cite_note-210]

On a trip to the United States in September 2021, Zelenskyy engaged in talks and commitments with U.S. president Joe Biden,[^cite_note-211] [Secretary of Defense](43998.md) [Lloyd Austin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lloyd_Austin), [Secretary of Energy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Secretary_of_Energy) [Jennifer Granholm](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jennifer_Granholm),[^cite_note-Ukrainian-212] and Secretary of State [Antony Blinken](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antony_Blinken).[^cite_note-213] President Zelenskyy and First Lady [Olena Zelenska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zelenska) also took part in the opening of the Ukrainian House in Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-Ukrainian-212] On the same trip, he met with [Apple](856.md) CEO [Tim Cook](46878774.md)[^cite_note-214] and with Ukrainians in senior positions at [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley) tech companies,[^cite_note-215] and spoke at Stanford University.[^cite_note-216] While Zelenskyy was still in the U.S., just after delivering a speech at the United Nations, an assassination attempt was made in Ukraine on [Serhiy Shefir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Serhiy_Shefir), his closest aide. Shefir was unhurt in the attack, although his driver was hospitalised with three bullet wounds.[^cite_note-217]

#### Prelude to the 2022 Russian invasion

Main article: [Prelude to the 2022 Russian invasion of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prelude_to_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

In April 2021, in response to Russian military build-up at the Ukrainian borders, Zelenskyy spoke to American president Joe Biden and urged NATO members to speed up [Ukraine's request for membership](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine%E2%80%93NATO_relations).[^cite_note-218]



Zelenskyy at the [58th Munich Security Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/58th_Munich_Security_Conference) in February 2022

On 26 November 2021, Zelenskyy accused Russia and Ukrainian oligarch [Rinat Akhmetov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rinat_Akhmetov) of backing a plan to overthrow his government.[^cite_note-219] Russia denied any involvement in a coup plot and Akhmetov said in a statement that "the information made public by Volodymyr Zelenskiy about attempts to draw me into some kind of coup is an absolute lie. I am outraged by the spread of this lie, no matter what the president's motives are."[^cite_note-220][^cite_note-221] In December 2021, Zelenskyy called for preemptive action against Russia.[^cite_note-222] On 19 January 2022, Zelenskyy said in a video message that the country's citizens should not panic and appealed to the media to be "methods of mass information and not mass hysteria."[^cite_note-223][^cite_note-buzzfeednews-embassy-224] On 28 January, Zelenskyy called on the West not to create a "panic" in his country over a potential Russian invasion, adding that constant warnings of an "imminent" threat of invasion are putting the economy of Ukraine at risk.[^cite_note-225] Zelenskyy said that "we do not see a bigger escalation" than in early 2021 when Russia's military build-up started.[^cite_note-226] Zelenskyy and U.S. president Joe Biden disagreed on how imminent the threat was.[^cite_note-227][^cite_note-228]

On 19 February, as worries of a Russian invasion of Ukraine grew, Zelenskyy warned the [Munich Security Conference](1464620.md) that Western nations should abandon their "appeasement" attitude toward Moscow. "Ukraine has been granted security assurances in exchange for giving up the world's third-largest nuclear arsenal. We don't have any firearms. And there's no security... But we have a right to urge a transformation from an appeasement policy to one that ensures security and peace," he stated.[^cite_note-f24msc-229]

### Since the 2022 Russian invasion

Main article: [Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](70149799.md)



[Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) chairman [Ruslan Stefanchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruslan_Stefanchuk), Ukrainian President Volodymyr Zelenskyy and Ukrainian Prime Minister [Denys Shmyhal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Denys_Shmyhal) after signing of the application for membership in the European Union during the war on 28 February 2022



President Volodymyr Zelenskyy visiting a military hospital for soldiers fighting in the [Kyiv Oblast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_offensive_\(2022\)), 13 March

#### First phase of the invasion

In the early hours of 24 February, shortly before the start of the Russian invasion, Zelenskyy recorded an address to the citizens of both Ukraine and Russia. He disputed claims of the Russian government about the presence of [neo-Nazis](54361.md#Ukraine) in the Ukrainian government and stated that he had no intention of attacking the Donbas region while highlighting his connections to the area.[^cite_note-230] In part of the address, he spoke in Russian to the people of Russia, appealing to them to pressure their leadership to prevent war:

> Who will suffer the most from this? People. Who does not want this more than anyone? People. Who can prevent this? People.
>
> Are these people present among you? I am sure there are. Public figures, journalists, musicians, actors, athletes, scientists, doctors, bloggers, stand-up comedians, Tik-Tokers and many more. Regular people. Regular, normal people. Men, women, the elderly, children, fathers, and most importantly, mothers. Just like people in Ukraine. Just like the authorities in Ukraine, no matter how much they try to convince you otherwise.
>
> I know that they will not show this appeal of mine on Russian television. But the citizens of Russia must see it. They must know the truth. And the truth is that this needs to stop before it is too late. And if the Russian leadership does not want to sit down at the table with us for the sake of peace, then perhaps, they will sit down at the table with you.
>
> [Do Russians want war?](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Do_the_Russians_Want_War%3F) I would very much like to answer this question. But the answer depends only on you, the citizens of the Russian Federation.

The speech was widely described as "emotional" and "astonishing."[^cite_note-231][^cite_note-232]

On the morning of 24 February, Putin [announced](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/On_conducting_a_special_military_operation) that Russia was initiating a "[special military operation](70156528.md)" in the Donbas. Russian missiles struck a number of military targets in Ukraine, and Zelenskyy declared martial law.[^cite_note-233] Zelenskyy also announced that [diplomatic relations with Russia](17446303.md) were being severed, effective immediately.[^cite_note-234] Later in the day, he announced general mobilisation.[^cite_note-235] On 25 February, Zelenskyy said that despite Russia's claim that it was targeting only military sites, civilian sites were also being hit.[^cite_note-236] In an early morning address that day, Zelenskyy said that his intelligence services had identified him as Russia's top target, but that he would remain in Kyiv and his family would stay in the country. "They want to destroy Ukraine politically by destroying the head of state," he said.[^cite_note-237]

In the early hours of 26 February, during the most significant assault by Russian troops on the capital of Kyiv, the United States government and Turkish president [Recep Tayyip Erdoğan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Recep_Tayyip_Erdo%C4%9Fan) urged Zelenskyy to evacuate to a safer location, and both offered assistance for such an effort. Zelenskyy turned down both offers and opted to remain in Kyiv with its defence forces, saying that "the fight is here \[in Kyiv]; [I need ammunition, not a ride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/I_need_ammunition,_not_a_ride)."[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239][^cite_note-\:5-240]

More than 90% of Ukrainians supported the actions of Zelenskyy,[^cite_note-241] including more than 90% in western and central Ukraine and more than 80% in Russian-speaking regions in eastern and southern Ukraine.[^cite_note-242] A [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center) poll found that 72% of Americans had confidence in Zelenskyy's handling of international affairs.[^cite_note-243]

Since the start of the invasion, Zelenskyy has reportedly been the target of [more than a dozen assassination attempts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assassination_attempts_on_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy); three were prevented by tips from Russian [FSB](385990.md) employees who opposed the invasion. Two of those attempts were carried out by the [Wagner Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wagner_Group), a Russian [paramilitary](146730.md) force, and the third by the [Kadyrovites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kadyrovites), the personal guard of [Chechen leader](10917818.md) [Ramzan Kadyrov](647880.md).[^cite_note-244] While speaking about Ukrainian civilians who were killed by Russian forces, Zelenskyy said:[^cite_note-245]

> We will not forgive. We will not forget. We will punish everyone who committed atrocities in this war... We will find every scum who was shelling our cities, our people, who was shooting the missiles, who was giving orders. You will not have a quiet place on this earth – except for a grave.



Zelenskyy giving a video address to the United Kingdom's parliament on 8 March 2022

Zelenskyy repeatedly called for direct talks with Putin,[^cite_note-negotiation-246] saying: "Good Lord, what do you want? Leave our land. If you don't want to leave now, sit down with me at the negotiating table. But not from 30 meters away, like with Macron and [Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz). I don't bite."[^cite_note-Wermus2022-247] Zelenskyy said he was "99.9% sure" that Putin thought the Ukrainians would welcome the invading forces with "flowers and smiles."[^cite_note-248]

On 7 March 2022, as a condition for ending the invasion, the Kremlin demanded Ukraine's [neutrality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country); recognition of [Crimea](163045.md), which had been annexed by Russia, as Russian territory; and recognition of the [self-proclaimed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Self-declared_states) [separatist](286527.md) republics of Donetsk and Luhansk as independent states.[^cite_note-249] On 8 March, Zelenskyy expressed willingness to discuss Putin's demands.[^cite_note-negotiation-246] Zelenskyy said he was ready for dialogue, but "not for capitulation."[^cite_note-250] He proposed a new collective security agreement for Ukraine with the United States, Turkey, France, Germany as an alternative to the country joining NATO.[^cite_note-251] Zelenskyy's [Servant of the People](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Servant_of_the_People) party said that Ukraine would not give up its claims on Crimea, Donetsk and Luhansk.[^cite_note-252] However, Zelenskyy said that Ukraine was considering giving the [Russian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language_in_Ukraine) protected minority status.[^cite_note-253]



Zelenskyy with Polish Prime Minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md), Czech Prime Minister [Petr Fiala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Fiala), Slovenian Prime Minister [Janez Janša](1038986.md) and Deputy Prime Minister of Poland [Jarosław Kaczyński](637440.md), Kyiv, 15 March

On 15 March 2022, [Polish](22936.md) Prime Minister [Mateusz Morawiecki](48505487.md), together with [Czech](5321.md) Prime Minister [Petr Fiala](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petr_Fiala) and [Slovenian](27338.md) Prime Minister [Janez Janša](1038986.md), visited [Kyiv](585629.md) to meet with Zelenskyy in a display of support for Ukraine.[^cite_note-254] On 16 March 2022, a [deepfake](56641018.md#Volodymyr_Zelenskyy) appeared online of Zelenskyy calling on Ukrainian citizens to surrender to Russia. The attack was largely deemed to have failed at its intended goal.[^cite_note-255] The video is considered to be the first use of deepfake technology in a global-scale [disinformation attack](65376326.md).[^cite_note-256]

Zelenskyy has made an effort to rally the governments of Western nations to isolate Russia. He has made numerous addresses to the legislatures of the EU,[^cite_note-257][^cite_note-258] UK,[^cite_note-259] Poland,[^cite_note-260] Australia,[^cite_note-261] Canada,[^cite_note-262] US,[^cite_note-263] Germany,[^cite_note-264] Israel,[^cite_note-265] Italy,[^cite_note-266] Japan,[^cite_note-267] the Netherlands,[^cite_note-268] Romania,[^cite_note-269] and the [Nordic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries).[^cite_note-270][^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272]



Zelenskyy in the Kyiv Oblast following the recapture of the region by Ukraine, 4 April

On 23 March, Zelenskyy was calling on Russians to [emigrate from Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_emigration_following_the_2022_invasion_of_Ukraine) so as not to finance the war in Ukraine with their taxes.[^cite_note-273] In March 2022, Zelenskyy supported the suspension of 11 Ukrainian political parties with ties to Russia: the [Socialist Party of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_Party_of_Ukraine), [Derzhava](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Derzhava_\(Ukrainian_party\)), Left Opposition, [Nashi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nashi_\(political_party\)), [Opposition Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opposition_Bloc_\(2019\)), Opposition Platform — For Life, [Party of Shariy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Party_of_Shariy), [Progressive Socialist Party of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Progressive_Socialist_Party_of_Ukraine), [Union of Leftists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_of_Leftists), and the [Volodymyr Saldo Bloc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volodymyr_Saldo_Bloc).[^cite_note-274][^cite_note-275][^cite_note-276] The [Communist Party of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Communist_Party_of_Ukraine), another pro-Russia party, had already been banned in 2015 because of its support to the [Donbas separatists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_separatist_forces_in_Donbas).[^cite_note-277] Zelenskyy also supported consolidating all TV news stations into a single 24-hour news broadcast run by the Ukrainian state during martial law.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-telemarathon_decree-279]

In May 2022, Zelenskyy said that Ukrainian men of [conscription](5735.md) age had a duty to remain in Ukraine.[^cite_note-280] As Zelenskyy ordered a [general military mobilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Ukrainian_mobilization) in February 2022, he also banned men aged 18 to 60 from leaving Ukraine.[^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282]



Zelenskyy awarding a soldier near the front line in the [Kharkiv Oblast](494901.md), 29 May

On 25 May 2022, Zelenskyy denounced suggestions by former US diplomat [Henry Kissinger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Kissinger) that Ukraine should [cede control of Crimea and Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_border_changes_\(1914%E2%80%93present\)) to Russia in exchange for peace.[^cite_note-283] He said that Ukraine would not agree to cease fire until Russia agreed to return Crimea and the Donbas region to Ukraine,[^cite_note-284] and that "Ukraine will fight until it regains all its territories".[^cite_note-285] However, he later said he did not believe that all the land seized by Russia since 2014 could be liberated by force, "If we decide to go that way, we will lose hundreds of thousands of people."[^cite_note-286]

On 30 May 2022, Zelenskyy criticised EU leaders for being too soft on Russia and asked, "Why can Russia still earn almost a billion euros a day by selling energy?"[^cite_note-287] The study published by the [Centre for Research on Energy and Clean Air](68658532.md) (CREA) calculates that the EU paid Russia about €56 billion for [fossil fuel deliveries](24648652.md) in the three months following the start of Russia's invasion.[^cite_note-288]

On 20 June 2022, Zelenskyy addressed [African Union](10634933.md) (AU) representatives via videoconference. He invited [African](5334607.md) leaders to a virtual meeting, but only four of them attended.[^cite_note-289] On 20 July 2022, South America's [Mercosur](25869317.md) trade bloc refused Zelenskyy's request to speak at the trade bloc's summit in Paraguay.[^cite_note-290]

#### Ukrainian counteroffensives and Russian annexations



Zelenskyy near the frontline in the [Kharkiv counteroffensive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Kharkiv_counteroffensive), 14 September 2022

Speaking about the [2022 Russian mobilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russian_mobilisation), Zelenskyy called on Russians to not submit to "criminal mobilisation", saying: "Russian commanders do not care about the lives of Russians – they just need to replenish the empty spaces left" by killed and wounded Russian soldiers.[^cite_note-291]

Following Putin's announcement of Russia [annexing four regions of Ukrainian territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts) it had seized during its invasion, Zelenskyy announced that Ukraine would not hold [peace talks with Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Russia%E2%80%93Ukraine_peace_negotiations) while Putin was president.[^cite_note-HardingKoshiw-292]

On 25 September 2022, Zelenskyy said that Putin's threats to use [nuclear weapons](21785.md) "could be a reality." He added that Putin "wants to scare the whole world" with [nuclear blackmail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nuclear_threats_during_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine).[^cite_note-293] He also said that Putin is aware that the "world will never forgive" a Russian nuclear strike.[^cite_note-294] When asked what kind of relationship Ukrainians and Ukraine will have with Russia after the war, Zelenskyy replied that "They took too many people, too many lives. The society will not forgive them," adding that "It will be the choice of our society whether to talk to them, or not to talk at all, and for how many years, tens of years or more."[^cite_note-295]

On 21 December 2022, Zelenskyy [visited the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_visit_by_Volodymyr_Zelenskyy_to_the_United_States) on his first foreign trip since the war began.[^cite_note-296][^cite_note-297] He met with President [Joe Biden](145422.md) and addressed [Congress](31756.md) delivering his full speech in English. The United States announced they would supply [Patriot missiles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Patriot_missiles) to Ukraine as had been requested.[^cite_note-298]

#### 2023



Zelenskyy at the [UN Security Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_members_of_the_United_Nations_Security_Council) in New York City on 20 September 2023

In early 2023, Zelenskyy signed a new law declaring that [desertion](488824.md), or "failure to report for duty without a valid reason," would result in up to 12 years in prison.[^cite_note-299][^cite_note-300] Over a year later in late 2024, the [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) passed bills decriminalising soldiers' first instance of desertion or [absence without leave](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Absence_without_leave) if they returned to their duty station,[^cite_note-first_desertion_babel-301] and allowing these soldiers to return to military service.[^cite_note-first_desertion_KI-302]

In May 2023, he visited the [International Criminal Court](14880.md) in [The Hague](30269.md) and said he would like to see Putin stand trial for [war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) committed during the war in Ukraine,[^cite_note-303] including the [crime of aggression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_of_aggression).[^cite_note-304]

By 1 June the [Battle of Bakhmut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Bakhmut) had been decided in Russia's favour.

On 19 September 2023, in a speech to the [UN General Assembly](31957.md), Zelenskyy called on neutral countries in Latin America, Africa and Asia to abandon their neutrality and support Ukraine.[^cite_note-305] In October 2023, after the [Hroza missile attack](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hroza_missile_attack), he criticised countries supporting Russia, saying "all those who help Russia circumvent [sanctions](70323386.md) are criminals."[^cite_note-306]

#### 2024

On 8 February 2024 Zelenskyy decommissioned General [Valery Zaluzhny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valery_Zaluzhny) as Commander-in-Chief of the Ukrainian Armed Forces, and promoted General [Oleksandr Syrskyi](69446333.md) in his stead.[^cite_note-bbc1-307] On 17 February Syrskyi said Ukrainian forces were withdrawing [from Avdiivka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Avdiivka_\(2023%E2%80%932024\)) to avoid encirclement.[^cite_note-308][^cite_note-309][^cite_note-310][^cite_note-311][^cite_note-312]

On 25 June 2024 Zelenskyy decommissioned Lt. Gen. [Yuriy Sodol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuriy_Sodol), who had been promoted in February to Syrskyi's now-vacant position. He installed Brig. Gen. [Andriy Hnatov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriy_Hnatov) to replace Sodol.[^cite_note-bbc2-313]



Zelenskyy with French President [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and U.S. President-elect [Donald Trump](4848272.md) in Paris on 7 December 2024

In April 2024, Zelenskyy signed a new [mobilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilisation_in_Ukraine) law to increase the number of troops.[^cite_note-314][^cite_note-315] He also signed into law a measure lowering Ukraine's army mobilisation age from 27 to 25.[^cite_note-316]

In June 2024, Zelenskyy said that China's [support for Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_and_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) would prolong the war in Ukraine. According to Zelenskyy, Russia used Chinese diplomats to undermine the [Ukraine peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit) in Switzerland.[^cite_note-317]

In July 2024, Zelenskyy criticised [Narendra Modi](444222.md)'s meeting with Putin on the same day that [Russian missile strikes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/8_July_2024_Ukraine_missile_strikes) hit the children's hospital [Okhmatdyt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Okhmatdyt) in Kyiv,[^cite_note-318] saying "It is a huge disappointment and a devastating blow to peace efforts to see the leader of the world's largest democracy hug the world's most bloody criminal in Moscow on such a day."[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320][^cite_note-321]

On 30 August 2024, Zelenskyy decommissioned Lt Gen [Mykola Oleshchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mykola_Oleshchuk), who had headed the [Ukrainian Air Force](1135769.md) since 2021, shortly after the death of [F-16](11642.md) pilot Col [Oleksiy Mes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksiy_Mes?action=edit\&redlink=1) during a Russian missile attack. Politician [Maryana Bezuhla](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maryana_Bezuhla) claimed the pilot's jet had been shot down by the friendly fire of an [anti-aircraft missile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-aircraft_missile), and the death is under investigation.[^cite_note-bbc3-322] Zelenskyy did not specify a reason for the dismissal, but said "we must...take care of all our warriors."[^cite_note-Zelenskyy_Telegram_Oleshchuk-323][^cite_note-bbc3-322] Zelenskyy also said: "I have decided to replace the commander of the Air Forces... I am eternally grateful to all our military pilots," the next day after the pilot's death.[^cite_note-rtr1-324]

In October 2024, Zelenskyy unveiled the [Victory Plan for Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Plan_for_Ukraine).[^cite_note-325] The plan included five points: one geopolitical, two military, one economic, and one related to national defence and safety.[^cite_note-326]

In December 2024, Zelenskyy resisted pressure from the Biden administration to [lower the conscription age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_conscription_crisis) to 18 to replace Ukraine's [battlefield losses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casualties_of_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War).[^cite_note-327] In February 2025, he said that Ukraine would introduce special military contracts for volunteers aged 18 to 24.[^cite_note-328]

On 4 September 2024, most of the [Shmyhal Government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shmyhal_Government) cabinet tendered their resignations while Zelenskyy considered his reshuffle.[^cite_note-cnn2-329][^cite_note-wapo2-330]

#### 2025

##### 2025 United States Oval Office meeting

Main article: [2025 Trump–Zelenskyy Oval Office meeting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump%E2%80%93Zelenskyy_Oval_Office_meeting)



[Donald Trump](4848272.md) and [JD Vance](53396477.md) arguing with Zelenskyy in the Oval Office

In February 2025, Zelenskyy [met with U.S. President Trump](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Trump-Zelenskyy_meeting) at the [White House](33057.md) to discuss a proposed agreement which would give the United States the right to extract rare minerals from Ukrainian soil. The meeting soon turned into a heated exchange with Trump and Vice President [JD Vance](53396477.md) against Zelenskyy. The planned lunch, discussions, and signing were all cancelled and Zelenskyy was evicted from the White House.[^cite_note-331][^cite_note-332]

In the aftermath of the meeting, the Trump administration suspended the provision of intelligence and military aid to Ukraine for around a week. The provision of aid to Ukraine was resumed after Zelenskyy agreed to an unconditional 30-day ceasefire, contingent on Russian approval (as Russia rejected the proposal, the ceasefire did not actually materialise).



Zelenskyy and U.S. President [Donald Trump](4848272.md) at [NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit) in [The Hague](30269.md), 25 June 2025

In June 2025, after talks with Zelenskyy at [The Hague NATO summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_The_Hague_NATO_summit), Trump said he was considering sending more [Patriot missile batteries](52024.md) to Ukraine to protect Kyiv from Russian attacks.[^cite_note-333]

##### 2025 anti-corruption protests

Main article: [2025 anti-corruption protests in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_anti-corruption_protests_in_Ukraine)

On 22 July 2025, despite calls to veto, Zelenskyy signed a controversial law that removed independence from two Ukrainian anti-corruption bodies, the [National Anti-Corruption Bureau of Ukraine](33096574.md) (NABU) and [Specialised Anti-Corruption Prosecutor's Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Specialised_Anti-Corruption_Prosecutor's_Office) (SAPO), and gave more power to the government's Prosecutor General,[^cite_note-334] Zelenskyy loyalist [Ruslan Kravchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ruslan_Kravchenko).[^cite_note-335] Critics of the law have said it may hurt Ukraine's efforts to join the European Union,[^cite_note-NBC-336] while EU officials have called the law a "step back".[^cite_note-AlJAzeera-337]

After widespread protest across multiple cities, Zelenskyy submitted a new draft law aimed at restoring the independence of NABU and SAPO. He stated the new bill would protect the agencies from Russian influence while preserving their independence, though no details were given. According to NABU, the proposal restores both bodies' full powers and safeguards.[^cite_note-338] The new bill was adopted by parliament and signed by Zelenskyy on 31 July 2025; Zelenskyy said it was "very important that the state listens to public opinion".[^cite_note-billsigned_pravda-339]



Zelenskyy and Commander-in-Chief [Oleksandr Syrskyi](69446333.md) with members of the [14th Mechanized Brigade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/14th_Mechanized_Brigade_\(Ukraine\)) on the [Kupiansk front](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kupiansk_front), 12 December 2025

In October 2025, Zelenskyy welcomed [US sanctions](2030237.md) against Russia's largest oil companies [Rosneft](1313566.md) and [Lukoil](1027881.md).[^cite_note-340]

In an interview published on 25 September, Zelenskyy suggested he would not seek a second term after the war ends.[^cite_note-341] On 15 December 2025, he said in an interview with [ZDF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ZDF) that he would run if the next election was held during the war, but was unsure if he would run in peacetime.[^cite_note-zdf-342]<sup>: 35\:06 </sup>

#### 2026

In January 2026, Zelenskyy voiced strong support for the [anti-government protests in Iran](81976180.md), describing them as an 'uprising' and calling on the international community to help the Iranian people achieve regime change.[^cite_note-343] Speaking at the [World Economic Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/56th_World_Economic_Forum) in Davos, Zelenskyy criticised the international community for "standing aside" while the protests were "[drowned in blood](82036668.md)".[^cite_note-344]

In April 2026, Zelenskyy met with German Chancellor [Friedrich Merz](1967626.md) in Berlin to sign a \$4.7 billion bilateral defence partnership agreement.[^cite_note-latimes_april2026-345][^cite_note-346] The pact focused on drone co-production, missile deliveries, and battlefield data sharing.[^cite_note-347] During the meeting, Zelenskyy also addressed personnel shortages in Ukraine, saying that the country faced an estimated 200,000 military desertions and two million draft-dodgers.[^cite_note-latimes_april2026-345] He noted the need for military personnel rotations and called for the return of [mobilisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mobilization_in_Ukraine)-age men who had, according to Zelenskyy, left Ukraine illegally.[^cite_note-NV_April2026-348][^cite_note-latimes_april2026-345] Merz said that Germany would cooperate with Ukraine to restrict the asylum process for military-age Ukrainian men and facilitate their repatriation.[^cite_note-349]

#### Calls for ceasefire and peace talks



Zelenskyy at a meeting with the UN Secretary-General, April 2022

Further information: [Peace negotiations in the Russo-Ukrainian war (2022–present)](70255183.md)

At the beginning of Russia's invasion, Zelenskyy said he was "ready for dialogue" but not "capitulation", and repeatedly called for direct talks with Putin.[^cite_note-negotiation-246] On 3 March 2022, he called for Putin to "sit down with me at the negotiating table. But not from 30 meters away, like with [Macron](43671127.md) and [Scholz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olaf_Scholz). I don't bite. What are you afraid of?". This was referring to [the long table](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vladimir_Putin's_meeting_table) Putin uses for meetings.[^cite_note-Wermus2022-247]

During the [early negotiations in March 2022](70255183.md#Early_negotiations_\(February%E2%80%93April_2022\)), Zelenskyy said his priorities were to stop the invasion, protect Ukraine's sovereignty, and for Ukraine to be given "real" security guarantees to prevent further Russian aggression.[^cite_note-350] In an interview with Russian journalists on 27 March 2022, Zelenskyy said he was willing to discuss Ukraine becoming a [neutral country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neutral_country), in exchange for security guarantees, and that he was willing to negotiate the status of Russian-occupied Crimea and the Donbas. He said there could be no peace deal without a ceasefire and Russian troop withdrawal. Russia's media watchdog [Roskomnadzor](15773661.md) ordered Russian media not to publish the interview.[^cite_note-351]

As the [talks in Istanbul](70255183.md#Istanbul_peace_talks) stalled, Zelenskyy tried to break the deadlock by proposing two separate treaties: an agreement on security guarantees "from those who are prepared to give such security guarantees", and another bilateral agreement between Ukraine and Russia. Several countries had shown willingness to become guarantors of Ukraine's security, but none had committed. Zelenskyy said "Moscow would like to have one treaty that would resolve all the issues. However, not everyone sees themselves at the table with Russia. ... people are saying, sorry, we have seen [what happened in Bucha](70455343.md), circumstances change".[^cite_note-352]



Zelenskyy and other world leaders at the [June 2024 Ukraine peace summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2024_Ukraine_peace_summit)

In November 2022, Zelenskyy proposed a [10-point peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukraine's_Peace_Formula).[^cite_note-kp10point-353][^cite_note-ajz1-354] The key points were:

- A ceasefire and withdrawal of Russian military forces from Ukraine

- Release of prisoners and return of [Ukrainian children deported to Russia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Child_abductions_in_the_Russo-Ukrainian_War)

- Protection and restoration of [Ukraine's energy infrastructure](71963495.md)

- Prosecution of [Russian war crimes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_crimes_in_the_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine)

- Security guarantees for Ukraine to prevent future aggression

Russia rejected Zelenskyy's peace plan and reiterated that it would not give up any territory it has taken by force.[^cite_note-ajz1-354]

&#x20;Zelenskyy accepted a proposal for a 30-day unconditional ceasefire in March 2025, which was supported by the US and European allies.[^cite_note-355] Russia repeatedly rejected the ceasefire proposal[^cite_note-356] and set out numerous conditions for halting its war.[^cite_note-357] On 1 June 2025, Zelenskyy said:

> We offered the Russians a ceasefire. Since March 11, the U.S. proposal for a full and unconditional ceasefire has been on the table. It was the Russians who chose to continue the war – even under conditions where the entire world is calling for an end to the killing. And pressure is truly needed – pressure on Russia that should bring it back to reality. Pressure through sanctions. Pressure from our forces. Pressure through diplomacy.[^cite_note-358]



Zelenskyy and other leaders at the [2025 London Summit on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_London_Summit_on_Ukraine)

On 15 May 2025, Russian and Ukrainian delegations held direct talks in [Istanbul](3391396.md), for the first time since early 2022. Zelenskyy arrived in Turkey several days before and said he was ready for face-to-face talks with Putin. Zelenskyy said "There is no point in prolonging the killings. And I will be waiting for Putin in Turkiye ... I hope that this time the Russians will not look for excuses". He also called for a ceasefire as a first step in negotiations. Putin rejected the call for a ceasefire[^cite_note-359] and turned down Zelenskyy's invitation.[^cite_note-360] The Russians would not compromise on their "maximalist" demands, and the talks failed.[^cite_note-361]

The US and Russia were [due to hold a peace summit in Budapest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2025_Russia%E2%80%93United_States_summit_in_Hungary), Hungary, in October 2025. US president Trump suggested that he would meet Putin and Zelenskyy separately at the summit.[^cite_note-362] Zelenskyy said he was willing to go to the summit and talk directly with Putin, asking "how can there be deals about us without us?".[^cite_note-363] Trump cancelled the summit, due to the Russians' refusal to compromise on their maximalist demands.[^cite_note-364][^cite_note-365]

Zelenskyy travelled to Turkey again in November 2025 in a bid to revive negotiations with Russia on ending the war. However, Russian representatives declined to attend.[^cite_note-366]

In November 2025, US president Trump adopted a [28-point peace plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._peace_plan_for_Ukraine), interpreted as broadly pro-Russian,[^cite_note-e610-367][^cite_note-l915-368] and according to *[The Insider](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Insider_\(website\))*, at its core a recycled Russian document substantially written by [Kirill Dmitriev](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kirill_Dmitriev).[^cite_note-j465-369] In late December 2025, Zelenskyy, his representatives, along with European allies and the US then produced revised versions of the peace plan, but these were rejected by the Russians.[^cite_note-370]

&#x20;In May 2026, Russia demanded a 48-hour ceasefire so that it could mark [Victory Day on 9 May](337670.md). The Russian defence ministry threatened a "massive missile strike on the centre of Kyiv" if its truce demand was not met. In response, Zelenskyy announced an unlimited ceasefire from 6 May, saying Ukraine would observe the ceasefire for as long as Russia did. However, Russia continued attacking Ukraine. Zelenskyy said:&#x20;

> They want Ukraine's permission to hold [their parade](83104217.md) – so they can safely take to [the square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Red_Square) for an hour once a year, and then go back to killing our people and waging war. The Russians are already talking about strikes after May 9. A strange and certainly twisted logic from the Russian leadership.[^cite_note-371]

## Political views

### Economic issues

In a June 2019 interview with [BIHUS info](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BIHUS_info?action=edit\&redlink=1), a [representative of the president of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representatives_of_the_President_of_Ukraine) at the [Cabinet of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabinet_of_Ministers_of_Ukraine), [Andriy Herus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andriy_Herus) stated that Zelenskyy had never promised to lower communal tariffs, but that a campaign video in which Zelenskyy stated that the price of [natural gas in Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_gas_in_Ukraine) could fall by 20–30% or maybe more was not a direct promise but actually "half-hinting" and "joking".[^cite_note-ZelenskyNP-372] Zelenskyy's election manifesto mentioned tariffs only once – that money raised from a capital amnesty would go towards "lowering the tariff burden on low-income citizens".[^cite_note-373][^cite_note-374]

### Foreign policy



Zelenskyy with world leaders who attended the [August 2025 White House multilateral meeting on Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/August_2025_White_House_multilateral_meeting_on_Ukraine)



Zelenskyy and Indian Prime Minister [Narendra Modi](444222.md) at the [COP26 climate summit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Nations_Climate_Change_conference) in Glasgow, November 2021

During his presidential campaign, Zelenskyy said that he supported Ukraine's joining the EU and NATO, but he said Ukrainian voters should decide on the country's membership of these two organisations in referendums.[^cite_note-Zelensky7209965-375][^cite_note-376] At the same time, he believed that the Ukrainian people had already chosen "eurointegration".[^cite_note-Zelensky7209965-375][^cite_note-377] Zelenskyy's electoral programme stated that Ukrainian NATO membership is "the choice of the [Maidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revolution_of_Dignity) and the course that is enshrined in the [Constitution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Ukraine), in addition, it is an instrument for strengthening our defence".[^cite_note-ZelenskyNATOFC-378] The programme stated that Ukraine should aim to apply for a [NATO Membership Action Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Membership_Action_Plan)[^cite_note-ZelenskyNATOFC-378] and apply for EU membership in 2024.[^cite_note-7094332roadUNEU-379] During his election campaign, Zelenskyy said that he wanted to build "a strong, powerful, free Ukraine, which is not the younger sister of Russia, which is not a corrupt partner of Europe, but our independent Ukraine."[^cite_note-380]

In February 2022, four days into the Russian invasion, he signed an application for [Ukraine to join the EU](5612265.md).[^cite_note-381][^cite_note-382] In September 2022, he signed the application for Ukraine to join NATO, after Russia proclaimed it had [annexed the country's southeast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_annexation_of_Donetsk,_Kherson,_Luhansk_and_Zaporizhzhia_oblasts).[^cite_note-HardingKoshiw-292] There is a lengthy process for joining both organisations.

Regarding [China](5405.md), Zelenskyy had tried to position Ukraine as a neutral party in the political and [trade tensions](57055272.md) between China and the United States. In January 2021, Zelenskyy said in an interview with *[Axios](52436164.md)* that he does not see China as a geopolitical threat and that he does not agree with the US assertions that it represents one.[^cite_note-383] After the Russian invasion, in May 2022, Zelenskyy said he was satisfied with [China's policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_and_the_2022_Russian_invasion_of_Ukraine) of staying away from the conflict.[^cite_note-384] Zelenskyy says that China has the economic leverage to pressure Putin to end the war—"without China, Putin's Russia is nothing"—but says China had too often been silent instead of acting for peace.[^cite_note-385]

Zelenskyy [criticised Israel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Israel%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations) for refusing to sanction Russia over its invasion of Ukraine and refusing to give Ukraine defensive weaponry.[^cite_note-386][^cite_note-387] Zelenskyy condemned [Hamas](13913.md)' [7 October 2023 attack on Israel](74998317.md), and said Israel had a right to self-defence.[^cite_note-388] As the [Gaza war](74998519.md) continued, Zelenskyy affirmed Ukraine's recognition of the [State of Palestine](241405.md) and support for a [two-state solution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two-state_solution); called for international law to be followed; and expressed Ukraine's readiness to send humanitarian aid to Gaza and desire to prevent civilian suffering.[^cite_note-KIGaza-389][^cite_note-NVGaza-390] In 2024, Ukraine sent 7,000 tons of wheat flour to the Palestinian territories, under the [Grain From Ukraine programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grain_From_Ukraine_program) launched by Zelenskyy.[^cite_note-391] In 2026, Zelenskyy threatened to sanction Israel for buying large shipments of [stolen Ukrainian wheat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_theft_of_Ukrainian_grain) from Russia.[^cite_note-392]

Zelenskyy spoke in support of [Azerbaijan](746.md) during the [Second Nagorno-Karabakh War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Nagorno-Karabakh_War) over the [disputed region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_territorial_disputes) of [Nagorno-Karabakh](65220.md). He said in October 2020, "We support Azerbaijan's territorial integrity and sovereignty just as Azerbaijan always supports our territorial integrity and sovereignty".[^cite_note-393]

#### Policy toward Russia and Donbas separatists

Zelenskyy supported the [Euromaidan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Euromaidan) movement in late 2013 and early 2014. During the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas_\(2014%E2%80%932022\)), he actively supported the Ukrainian army.[^cite_note-0396374-zelenskiy-51] Zelenskyy helped fund a [volunteer battalion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_volunteer_battalions_\(since_2014\)) fighting in Donbas.[^cite_note-394] In a 2014 interview with *[Komsomolskaya Pravda v Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Komsomolskaya_Pravda_v_Ukraine)*, Zelenskyy said that he would have liked to pay a visit to Crimea, but would avoid it because "armed people are there."[^cite_note-395] In August 2014, Zelenskyy performed for Ukrainian troops in [Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariupol) and later his studio donated ₴1 million to the Ukrainian army.[^cite_note-396] Regarding the [2014 Russian annexation of Crimea](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Annexation_of_Crimea_by_the_Russian_Federation), Zelenskyy said that it might only be possible to return Crimea to Ukrainian control only after a [regime change](154074.md) in Russia.[^cite_note-397]

In an interview in December 2018 with *Ukrainska Pravda*, Zelenskyy stated that as president he would try to end the ongoing war in Donbas by negotiating with Russia.[^cite_note-8/12/26/7202291/_Zelensky-398] As he considered the leaders of the [Donetsk People's Republic](42425747.md) and the [Luhansk People's Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luhansk_People's_Republic) (DPR and LPR) to be Russia's "puppets," it would "make no sense to speak with them."[^cite_note-8/12/26/7202291/_Zelensky-398] He did not rule out holding a referendum on the issue.[^cite_note-8/12/26/7202291/_Zelensky-398] In an interview published three days before the 2019 presidential election (on 21 April), Zelenskyy stated that he was against granting the Donbas region "special status."[^cite_note-3daysbUpeZi-399] In the interview he also said that if he were elected president he would not sign a law on amnesty for the militants of the DPR and LPR.[^cite_note-3daysbUpeZi-399]



Zelenskyy, French president [Emmanuel Macron](43671127.md) and Russian president [Vladimir Putin](32817.md) meeting in Paris on 9 December 2019 in the "[Normandy Format](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Normandy_Format)" aimed at ending the [war in Donbas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/War_in_Donbas_\(2014%E2%80%932022\)).&#x20;

In response to suggestions to the contrary, he stated in April 2019 that he regarded Putin "as an enemy."[^cite_note-400] On 2 May 2019, Zelenskyy wrote on Facebook that "the border is the only thing Russia and Ukraine have in common."[^cite_note-401]

Zelenskyy opposed the [Nord Stream 2](50335419.md) natural gas pipeline between Russia and Germany, calling it "a dangerous weapon, not only for Ukraine but for the whole of Europe."[^cite_note-402]

### Government reform

During the 2019 presidential campaign, Zelenskyy promised bills to fight [corruption](33085862.md), including removal of immunity from the [president](20688479.md), [members](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People's_Deputy_of_Ukraine) of the [Verkhovna Rada](844993.md) (Ukraine's national parliament) and judges, a law about impeachment, reform of election laws, and providing efficient trial by jury. He promised to bring the salary for military personnel "to the level of [NATO](21133.md) standards."[^cite_note-403]

Although Zelenskyy had earlier stated that he preferred elections with [open list](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_list) election ballots (such as in his inauguration speech),[^cite_note-inaugurationspeech-129] after he called the snap [2019 Ukrainian parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election), his draft law "On amendments to some laws of Ukraine in connection with the change of the electoral system for the election of people's deputies" proposed to hold the election with closed lists because the 60-day term to the snap election did not "leave any chances for the introduction of this system."[^cite_note-UPclosedlistsrejected-130] However, this bill was rejected by parliament.[^cite_note-uawireclosedlists-128]

### Social issues

In 2014, Zelenskyy said he opposed targeting the Russian language in Ukraine and banning artists for their political opinions (such as those viewed by the Government as [anti-Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-Ukrainian_sentiment)).[^cite_note-sevas.com358742-73][^cite_note-404] As a presidential candidate in April 2019, he stated that he was not against a [Ukrainian language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language) quota ([on radio and TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainization#Mass_media)), although he noted the quotas could be tweaked.[^cite_note-/7212672/_Zelensky-405] He also said that Russian artists "who have turned into (anti-Ukrainian) politicians" should remain banned from entering Ukraine.[^cite_note-3daysbUpeZi-399]

In October 2019, during his record 14-hour press conference, Zelenskyy was interrupted by a [homophobic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homophobic) heckler and responded with irritation that he would not say anything negative about the [LGBTQ](78174325.md) community, because "we all live in an open society where each one can choose the language they speak, their ethnicity and [\[sexual\] orientation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sexual_orientation). Leave those finally at peace, for God’s sake!"[^cite_note-atlantic_betrayal-406][^cite_note-atlantic_14h-138][^cite_note-pinknews_heckler-407][^cite_note-npr_lgbtq-408] In response to an August 2022 petition demanding equal rights for same-sex couples, Zelenskyy affirmed that democracies were measured by how they ensure equal rights for all citizens, that "all people are free and equal in their dignity and rights",[^cite_note-LGBTNYT-409] and that the family "consists of persons who live together, are connected by common life, have mutual rights and obligations";[^cite_note-lgbtcnn-410] he asked the [Prime Minister of Ukraine](1256678.md) to review [civil partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_union) for same-sex couples and called this "part of the work on establishing and ensuring human rights and freedoms".[^cite_note-lgbtcnn-410] However, he said that [same-sex marriage](92656.md) could not be introduced during wartime as this would require amending the [Constitution of Ukraine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Ukraine), which defines marriage as "based on the free consent of a woman and a man", and the Constitution cannot be changed during martial law.[^cite_note-LGBTNYT-409][^cite_note-411][^cite_note-blade-412] Civil rights organisations such as [Kyiv Pride](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kyiv_Pride) praised the statement,[^cite_note-blade-412] though [Holos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holos_\(political_party\)) MP [Inna Sovsun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inna_Sovsun) criticised the lack of details about legal proposals for civil partnerships.[^cite_note-LGBTNYT-409]

On 2 December 2022, Zelenskyy said his administration would enter a bill in the Verkhovna Rada that would ban "religious organizations affiliated with centers of influence in the Russian Federation", referring to the [Ukrainian Orthodox Church (Moscow Patriarchate)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Orthodox_Church_\(Moscow_Patriarchate\)) (UOC-MP), from operating in Ukraine.[^cite_note-413] The bill was passed by parliament and signed by Zelenskyy in August 2024,[^cite_note-uocmp-414] after opposition parties protested the bill's delay.[^cite_note-uocmpdelay-415] The law treats each parish individually and gives it nine months to cut ties with the UOC-MP, following which a special commission will inspect individual parishes and file lawsuits against non-compliant ones; the court can then decide on a ban in each case.[^cite_note-uocmpexplain-416]

In 2024, Zelenskyy said he would sign a law introducing [civil partnerships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_partnership) in Ukraine if parliament passed it; however, that law remains stalled in parliament as of 2026.[^cite_note-ki_lgbt-417]

In June 2026, [Oleksandr Demenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleksandr_Demenko), a [veteran of Mariupol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Mariupol) and head of the [non-governmental organisation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-governmental_organisation) [LGBT Military for Equal Rights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LGBT_Military_for_Equal_Rights?action=edit\&redlink=1), asked Zelenskyy during a cultural event whether Ukraine needed cultural products that would increase tolerance and normalise the LGBT community in Ukraine. In response, Zelenskyy spoke in favour of open public dialogue about LGBT rights in Ukraine: "I believe we need to speak openly with society about everything, and that is absolutely normal. We are all here with you, we are defending the state, we are equal and we have absolutely equal rights — regardless of any, I don’t know, prejudices of people from the 15th century. You and I are modern people."[^cite_note-ki_lgbt-417][^cite_note-nv_lgbt-418][^cite_note-interfax_lgbt-419]

## Public image and assessments



Zelenskyy on [Holodomor Memorial Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holodomor_Memorial_Day) in [Dormition Cathedral, Kyiv Pechersk Lavra](78734654.md), 2023

A poll in May 2021 by the [Rating Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rating_\(sociological_group\)) gave Zelenskyy the highest trust rating out of all Ukrainian presidents, and ranked him as the second-best president after [Leonid Kuchma](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Kuchma).[^cite_note-Liga2020-22][^cite_note-up_kuchma-24]

Zelenskyy has gained worldwide recognition as the wartime leader of Ukraine during the Russian invasion; historian [Andrew Roberts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrew_Roberts_\(historian\)) compared him to [Winston Churchill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Winston_Churchill).[^cite_note-deutsche-welle-420][^cite_note-421] *[Harvard Political Review](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Harvard_Political_Review)* said that Zelenskyy "has harnessed the power of social media to become history's first truly online wartime leader, bypassing traditional gatekeepers as he uses the internet to reach out to the people."[^cite_note-422] He has been described as a [national hero](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_hero) or a "global hero" by many commentators, including publications such as *[The Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hill_\(newspaper\))*, *[Deutsche Welle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deutsche_Welle)*, *[Der Spiegel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Der_Spiegel)* and *[USA Today](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USA_Today)*.[^cite_note-deutsche-welle-420][^cite_note-thehill-423][^cite_note-424][^cite_note-425] [BBC News](1139893.md) and *[The Guardian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Guardian)* have reported that his response to the invasion has received praise even from previous critics.[^cite_note-\:5-240][^cite_note-426]

In October 2022, a Rating Group poll ranked Zelenskyy the second most outstanding Ukrainian of all time, behind [Taras Shevchenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taras_Shevchenko).[^cite_note-rating_outstanding-29][^cite_note-up_outstanding-30][^cite_note-lb_outstanding-31] The same poll in August 2026 ranked him the fifth most outstanding Ukrainian of all time.[^cite_note-prm_outstanding_2026-27][^cite_note-komersant_outstanding_2026-28][^cite_note-rating_outstanding_2026-26]

Zelenskyy's presidency has faced domestic and international criticism over media restrictions, the centralisation of power [during wartime](70149799.md), and corruption allegations involving figures close to his administration. In February 2021, Zelenskyy imposed sanctions on several opposition-supporting television and media companies, which his government accused of being financed by Moscow and linked to pro-Russian politician [Viktor Medvedchuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Viktor_Medvedchuk). The measure was supported by some Western partners as a response to Russian influence, but the [United Nations human rights office](22463.md) later said that Ukraine's closure of television channels and online media outlets was "not in line with international human rights law".[^cite_note-427][^cite_note-428]

During the Russian invasion, martial law postponed elections and restricted political activity, media operations, freedom of movement and public assembly. The *[Associated Press](18935732.md)* described this as a wartime paradox in which Ukraine, while defending itself as a democracy, had suspended or limited some democratic norms. *[Reuters](18998750.md)* reported in April 2025 that opposition politicians, including former president [Petro Poroshenko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petro_Poroshenko), had begun to accuse Zelenskyy of using martial law to strengthen presidential power, although Poroshenko's party largely supported extending martial law because of continuing Russian attacks.[^cite_note-429][^cite_note-430]

Zelenskyy's anti-corruption record has also been scrutinised. *Reuters* reported in 2023 that political opponents and anti-corruption campaigners had accused powerful figures of shielding deputy presidential chief of staff [Oleh Tatarov](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oleh_Tatarov) from prosecution; Tatarov denied wrongdoing, and a case against him had been closed on procedural grounds.[^cite_note-431]

In 2026, Reuters reported that a corruption investigation had reached Zelenskyy's inner circle after former chief of staff [Andriy Yermak](63086056.md) was named as a suspect in an alleged \$10.5 million money-laundering scheme. Zelenskyy was not named in the investigation, and Ukraine's anti-corruption bureau said he was not a subject of any probe. Reuters described the case as a potential longer-term reputational risk for Zelenskyy, while noting that some lawmakers saw the investigation as evidence that Ukraine's anti-corruption institutions remained functional.[^cite_note-432]

## Personal life



Zelenskyy with his wife [Olena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zelenska) voting in the [2019 parliamentary election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Ukrainian_parliamentary_election)

In September 2003, Zelenskyy married [Olena Kiyashko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olena_Zelenska), with whom he had attended school and university.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-TheEconomist-434] Kiyashko worked as a scriptwriter at Kvartal 95.[^cite_note-435] The couple has two children: their first, daughter Oleksandra, was born in July 2004, and their second, son Kyrylo, in January 2013. In Zelenskyy's 2014 film *8 New Dates*, their daughter played Sasha, the daughter of the protagonist. In 2016, she participated in the show *Make the Comedian Laugh: Kids* and won ₴50,000.[^cite_note-LIGAZelenskybio-33] The family, which lives in Kyiv,[^cite_note-TheEconomist-434] has two dogs, a cat, a parrot, and a [guinea pig](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinea_pig).[^cite_note-436]

Zelenskyy's assets were worth about ₴37 million (about US\$1.5 million) in 2018.[^cite_note-437]

Zelenskyy's first language is [Russian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Russian_language). He became fluent in [Ukrainian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_language)[^cite_note-npr_fluent-438][^cite_note-439] later in life;[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 157 </sup> he hired a tutor to improve his Ukrainian in 2017,[^cite_note-Derix-52]<sup>: 74–75 </sup>[^cite_note-440][^cite_note-afr_tutor-441] and said in November 2019 after becoming president that he would like to improve his Ukrainian further.[^cite_note-442] He also speaks [English](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/English_language),[^cite_note-443][^cite_note-444][^cite_note-445] and observers have noted that his command of English has improved during the full-scale war.[^cite_note-japantimes_english-446][^cite_note-abc_english-447]

## Achievements, awards, and recognition

### Teletriumph Awards

The [Teletriumph Awards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teletriumph_Awards) are the national television awards of Ukraine. Zelenskyy has won this award over 30 times, in multiple categories, in multiple years.[^cite_note-448] However, Teletriumph 2018 remains the most recent award year; the awards were cancelled in 2019 due to reorganisational efforts,[^cite_note-449] and again the following year due to COVID-19. In 2021, Zelenskyy served on the "Expert Council" of the [Ukrainian Television Academy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ukrainian_Television_Academy?action=edit\&redlink=1) (UTVA), to oversee the restructuring efforts of the awards and the discussions for a new nomination process.[^cite_note-450] As the television academy was finalising its award nominations for 2022, Russia invaded Ukraine. The awards are not considered permanently cancelled, but indefinitely suspended.

See also: [Polish Order of the White Eagle controversy](83538418.md)

### Species named after Zelenskyy

*[Ausichicrinites zelenskyyi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ausichicrinites_zelenskyyi)*, an extinct species of [feather star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feather_star) described on 20 July 2022 by a group of Polish [palaeontologists](23084.md), is named after Zelenskyy "for his courage and bravery in defending free Ukraine."[^cite_note-492][^cite_note-493]

### Other media

In 2023, American [country music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Country_music) singer [Brad Paisley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brad_Paisley) released a song titled "[Same Here](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Same_Here)", which features Zelenskyy in a spoken-word interlude.[^cite_note-ap-494]
